From:

Subject: UDC-23-00065-01 | 5224 Ridge Road – Zoning Map Amendment  
Date: 
April 5, 2023 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Zoning Map Amendment (zone change).  This is approved by Ordinance.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
4/10/2023 
• 
Public Services Committee  
4/18/2023 
• 
Second reading  
 
4/24/2023 
• 
Public Services Committee  
5/2/2023 
• 
Third and final reading   
5/8/2023 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their April 3, 2023 meeting and unanimously recommended 
the Governing Body approve this application.  
 
The Planning Commission staff report is attached.  The applicant, John Lopeman, for the Cheyenne 
Classical Academy has withdrawn their application for a Primary and Secondary School.  The school has 
found another location for their development.  The zone change and annexation will move forward 
being represented by City Staff.  There is not a proposed change of use at this time. 
 
Related Items: 
 
• 
UDC-23-00065:  Annexation

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE ESTABLISHING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION OF MR 
MEDIUM-DENSITY RESIDENTIAL FOR LAND BEING ANNEXED TO THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE LOCATED AT 5224 RIDGE ROAD (WEST OF RIDGE 
ROAD, BETWEEN JACKSON STREET AND DOUGLAS STREET). " 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a zoning map 
amendment for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code ("UDC"). 
 
Section 2.  That on April 3, 2023, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of this ordinance, and 
recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved, and the zoning classification of the real property described as: 
Tract 12, Dell Range Addition, a subdivision of part of the west one-half of Section 22, Township 
14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County Wyoming less that portion described 
in warranty deed recorded in Book 1148 Page 1486 and less that portion being the north 120 feet 
of the east half of Tract 12, Dell Range Addition, records of Laramie County, Wyoming 
is hereby established as MR Medium-density Residential. 
 
Section 5.  That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official 
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map amendment as 
described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If 
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases. 
 
Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication.

UDC-23-00065-01 
 
 
April 3, 2023 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of a portion of Tract 12, Dell Range Addition to MR Medium-density 
Residential as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria 
for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
UDC Article 5 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from County MR Medium-density Residential to 
City MR Medium-density Residential for 1.29 acres of land.  The land is currently occupied by a religious 
facility.  The proposed MR zoning allows for a variety of residential uses in a more compact neighborhood 
development pattern.  
 
The proposed MR zoning allows for primary and secondary schools with a Conditional Use approval.  A 
Conditional Use for a primary and secondary school was approved by the Board of Adjustment on March 16, 
2023. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure.

UDC-23-00065-01 
 
 
April 3, 2023 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property and the surrounding area is Urban 
Residential. According to comprehensive plan there are many optimal City zones for the subject 
property’s future land use designation, including MR Medium-density Residential.  
 
The policies of PlanCheyenne are supportive of this proposed zone change. The future land use 
plan encourages a variety of residential development patterns and the MR zone allows a wide 
variety of residential uses.  The future land use plan for the Urban Residential area encourages 
complementary uses, such as schools.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
All adjacent property is currently within the City limits and zoned MR.  Property to the north and 
east are existing single-family residential.  Property to the south and west are currently used for 
a Church.  The site will be replated and combined with the existing lots to the south and west for 
the proposed primary and secondary school.  MR is compatible with the area. The proposed 
zoning is also compatible with the future land use designation of Urban Residential.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The current zone is a County zone and a City zone is required to develop within City limits.  The 
City zone requested is the same as the County zone.  The MR zone in both the City and the 
County are intended to develop with a variety of residential uses.  Primary and secondary 
schools are a permitted use by right in the County Zone but require a Conditional Use approval 
in the City zone. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The zone change is proposed in association with a proposed redevelopment, a platting action 
and annexation. The associated platting action will conform to Article 4 standards to the extent 
possible. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 

UDC-23-00065-01 
 
 
April 3, 2023 
The MR zone is intended to develop with a mix of density and dwelling types within a 
neighborhood.  It is intended  for areas where more compact neighborhoods  development 
patterns are desired and specifically the Urban Residential Category in the PlanCheyenne. This 
area is designated as Urban Residential on the Future Land Use Plan. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
A primary and secondary school is proposed with this development.  A primary and secondary 
school is allowed by Conditional Use in the MR zone district.  A Conditional Use has been 
approved for a primary and secondary school on March 16, 2023.  The proposed school should 
have no issues meeting standards for the MR zone district.  The site could be used for a wide 
variety of housing options if it were not to develop into a school. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Development – Seth Lloyd – Proposal is consistent with the comprehensive plan. Proposal matches the 
surrounding properties and the current County zone. Appears to meet all review criteria. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d). The MR zone is 
appropriate based on the comprehensive plan and context of the property. 

UDC-23-00065-01 
 
 
April 3, 2023 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from County MR Medium-density 
Residential to City MR Medium-density Residential. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM 
MR 
MEDIUM-DENSITY 
RESIDENTIAL 
TO 
NR-2 
NEIGHBORHOOD RESIDENTIAL – MEDIUM DENSITY FOR LAND 
LOCATED ON LOTS 1 AND 2, BLOCK 1, TWIN 22 SUBDIVISION." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
 
Section 2.  That on April 3, 2023, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
 
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Lot 1 and 2, Block 1, Twin 22 Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby changed 
to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density. 
 
Section 5.  That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

UDC-23-00066 
 
 
April 3, 2023 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Twin 22 Subdivision to NR-2 Neighborhood 
Residential – Medium Density as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets 
the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
UDC Article 5 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from MR Medium-Density Residential to NR-2 
Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density for 0.18 acres of land. The land is currently vacant and is 
made up of two parcels under the same ownership. The properties are anticipated to have a single-family 
dwelling built on each lot.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. Plan Cheyenne also encourages infill development (although it is not the 
intent of the plan to change existing, stable neighborhoods).   
 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Urban Residential and the surrounding area 
is Urban Residential and Mixed-Use Residential. According to the comprehensive plan the

UDC-23-00066 
 
 
April 3, 2023 
optimal City zones for the subject property’s future land use designation is any one of the 
residential zones, including NR-2. 
 
Based on the policies and future land use map, NR-2 zoning is appropriate at this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
Nearby properties to the north, west, and south are zoned MR. MR and NR-2 zones are medium-
density residential zones. The difference is that NR-2 zoning anticipates alley-loaded 
development, allows smaller lots, and has a higher design standard. NR-2 zoning is compatible 
with surrounding zoning.  
 
Property to the east is zoned CB and is a part of a commercial corridor along a major 
throughfare. The Comprehensive Plan encourages commercial activities which serve 
neighborhoods, the community, and region. The Comprehensive Plan encourages the blending 
of CB zones with nearby neighborhoods in terms of scale, design, signage, and lighting. The NR-2 
zone allows for slightly more density which can help aid the transition from the CB zone into the 
residential neighborhood. NR-2 zoning is compatible with surrounding zoning.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The MR, as put forward in the UDC, is intended for larger-block, front-loaded, suburban style 
development patterns (like Saddle Ridge or The Pointe). NR-2 zoning is intended for smaller-
block, rear-loaded, compact development patterns (like what is found in the Avenues). 
Unfortunately, after the adoption of the UDC, a comprehensive zoning map amendment was 
not pursued, or neighborhoods like this one may have been changed to the new NR-2 zone. In 
short, the proposed NR-2 zone is more consistent with the existing development pattern of this 
neighborhood than the current MR zone. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The zone change is occurring in a pre-existing neighborhood. No changes to the street network 
are proposed. The neighborhood meets the smaller block sizes anticipated for NR-2 zoning.  
 
Accesses will not be changed with the zone change.  
 
Infrastructure (streets and utilities) exist in the area. 
 

UDC-23-00066 
 
 
April 3, 2023 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The NR-2 zone is intended for a medium density and mix of dwelling types that promote a 
consistent neighborhood character, with a compact development pattern and highly connected 
street network. The existing neighborhood meets these objectives. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The NR-2 zone allows a variety of residential uses (as does the current MR zone). The applicant 
intends to build two single family homes on DD8 lots which is permitted in the NR-2 zone.  
Should this particular project not move forward with the two single-family homes, the NR-2 
zoning provides a few more options for infill residential development. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
Proposal meets comprehensive plan principles. The entire area should be this zone based on the 
physical form of the neighborhood 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, review comments were received expressing the appropriateness of the  
NR-2 zone at this location. 
 

UDC-23-00066 
 
 
April 3, 2023 
 
 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The NR-2 zone 
is appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, and surrounding development 
patterns. 
 
 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from MR to NR-2. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Maps

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
April 5, 2023 
SUBJECT: 
Ordinance amending sections 2.04.160(D)&(E) and 12.04.180(B) of the 
City Code, in reference to Contract Approval Thresholds 
______________________________________________ 
Recently passed State of Wyoming Legislation, specifically Senate File SF0164 ‘Public 
improvement contract requirements-amendments.’ increased the bid threshold at which public 
improvement contracts must be advertised for bid from thirty-five thousand dollars ($35,000.00) 
to seventy-five thousand dollars ($75,000.00) – Effective April 1, 2023. 
 
The proposed Ordinance revises section City Code Section 2.04.160(D), Subsections 4 & 6, to 
set an approval limit of $75,000.00 for contracts that generate revenue and contracts for 
professional services.  Contracts for public improvements are covered under Subsection 1, and 
Wyoming Statute 15-1-113 sets the threshold for contracts of this nature ($75,000.00 as a result 
of SF0164). 
 
Changes to section 2.04.160(E) propose raising the threshold for Contract Modifications that do 
not require Governing Body approval from $50,000.00 to $75,000.00 (Applies only to Contracts 
previously approved by the Governing Body). 
 
Changes to section 12.04.180(B) propose raising the threshold to $75,000.00 for continuity across 
all thresholds previously established at $35,000.00. 
 
A table showing the existing thresholds, with their proposed changes, is included for refence. 
 
Although not enacted via this proposed Ordinance, changes to the City Purchasing Policy will be 
implemented upon approval of this Ordinance to update and revise the quote and bidding 
thresholds.  Most notably, the three-quote threshold range will change from $5,000 - $35,000, to 
$10,000 - $75,000. 
 
Funding:  
No Financial Impact

ORDINANCE NO. _________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING CHAPTER 2.04, MAYOR AND CITY 
COUNCIL, WITHIN TITLE 2, ADMINISTRATION AND PERSONNEL, 
AND CHAPTER 12.04, STREETS, SIDEWALKS AND DRIVEWAYS 
GENERALLY, WITHIN TITLE 12, STREETS, SIDEWALKS AND PUBLIC 
PLACES, OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, TO AMEND PROVISION GOVERNING CONTRACT 
APPROVALS" 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 160, Readings—Committee reports—Amendments, of Chapter 
2.04, Mayor and City Council, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, and Chapter 12.04, 
Streets, Sidewalks and Driveways Generally, within Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, 
is hereby amended as set out in this Ordinance. Sections currently existing and not set out within 
this ordinance remain unchanged. 
 
2.04.160 - Readings; committee reports; amendments; contract approval. 
 
D.  Contracts requiring governing body approval prior to execution by the mayor are as 
follows: 
 
1.  Public improvements in excess of the amount proscribed by state statutes; 
 
2.  Purchase of new motor vehicles; 
 
3.  Franchise agreements; 
 
4.  Those which generate revenue for the city in excess of seventy-five thousand dollars 
($75,000.00); 
 
5.  For the sale or trade of city property as specified in state statute; 
 
6.   Except that contracts for professional services for seventy-five thousand dollars 
($75,000.00) or less shall not require governing body approval prior to execution by 
the mayor; 
 
E.  Notwithstanding the requirements imposed under subsection (D) of this section, any 
contract previously approved by the governing body shall not require further governing 
body approval for the contract to be later modified to effectuate a project completion date 
extension or for a modification that increases the original contract price by less than fifty 
thousand dollars ($50,000.00), subject to the following conditions: 
 
3:36 pm, Apr 04 2023

 
 
Page 2 of 3 
1.  Governing body approval shall be required when the additive sum of one (1) or more 
contract modifications will exceed fifty thousand dollars ($50,000.00); and 
 
2.   Any contract modification to be executed without governing body approval in 
accordance with this subsection that increases the contract price shall not increase the 
total price of original contract by more than twenty percent (20%). 
 
F.  Any contract or contract modification that does not require governing body approval prior 
execution under subsections (D) or (E) of this section or section 12.04.180(B) shall be 
reviewed by the department of the city treasurer and the city attorney before the contract 
may be executed by the mayor. 
 
12.04.180 - Governing body may contract for construction. 
 
B.  Special Provision for Downtown Development District. The director of public works or the 
city engineer are empowered to let contracts for less than seventy-five thousand dollars 
($75,000.00) for the repair or replacement of curb and gutter located within the downtown 
development district on a rotating list of qualified contractors pursuant to a qualification 
process approved by the governing body and the purchasing manager. The requirements of 
subsection 2.04.160(D) shall not apply to contracts let pursuant to this process. 
 
Section 3.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases.  
 
Section 4.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-04-04_TotalExtraction_45306937.pdf
 
 
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
The Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and City of Cheyenne Council Members 
FROM: 
 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
 
March 24, 2023 
SUBJECT: 
Re-Appropriation to be considered for approval 
 
 
On April 10, 2023, the Governing Body will be referring a resolution to the Finance Committee to 
re-appropriate funds for the Fiscal Year (FY) 2023 City of Cheyenne Budget per W.S. § 16-4-112 
and 16-4-113.  Below is more detailed information regarding this re-appropriation request.  
 
GENERAL FUND – RESERVES REQUEST 
The following General Fund reserve requests are included for your consideration: 
Contract public defender fees.  The Mayor is requesting additional General Fund reserves in the 
amount of $90,400 for contract public defender fees.  This will cover the shortage in this line item 
for our contract public defenders.  The current budget is $110,400; and to date, we have spent a 
total of $157,900 in contract public defender fees.  In addition to this amount, there are five more 
months of expenses to pay (February and March will be $10,200 per month, and then for April, 
May, and June, the cost will drop down to $7,500 per month). 
Salary and benefits for new City Public Defender.  The City Attorney has received approval 
from Mayor Collins to hire a new Public Defender who will assist with current caseloads and 
reduce the costs of contracting public defender services. This position will be paid out of the 
Mayor’s budget.  The City intends to employ a local attorney to fill this position; once hired, this 
individual is scheduled to begin on May 1, 2023.  Therefore, the City Attorney is requesting 
$18,955 from General Fund reserves to cover salary and benefits for May and June. 
Salary and benefits for new Senior Accountant.  I am requesting $33,173 from General Fund 
reserves to cover salary and benefits of a new Senior Accountant position for the remaining three 
months of FY 2023.  The Mayor approved this position and I am pleased to announce the hiring 
of Sarah Crone as the City’s new Senior Accountant.  She is a Certified Public Accountant and is 
currently employed by MHP, LLP.  She serves as the in-charge supervisor of the City’s annual 
audit and has considerable experience in governmental accounting.  She will start on April 3, 2023. 

Sarah will assist the City Treasurer’s Department with the OpenGov software conversion, as this 
will be a massive undertaking.  As you will recall, the City Treasurer’s Department will implement 
five modules, including Financials, Budgeting and Planning, Procurement, Cartegraph Work 
Management and Financial Transparency, over a two-year period starting this fiscal year.   
Additionally, in December 2022, Congress approved the “Financial Data Transparency Act”, 
which is now an unfunded mandate that requires state and local governments to face time-
consuming and costly new requirements for sharing financial information with the public by 
putting their financial information in a standardized, machine-readable, and searchable format.  
This is going to be a significant undertaking for the City of Cheyenne and current staffing levels 
in the City Treasurer’s Department do not support these additional responsibilities.   
Salary and benefits for new DDA Administrator and Marketing Manager.  The Governing 
Body approved a Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) between the City and the Downtown 
Development Authority (DDA) on March 13, 2023, and as a result, the Planning and Development 
Director is requesting to hire an Administrator and Marketing Manager for the DDA.  The 
Administrator is projected to start approximately on April 15, 2023, and the Marketing Manager 
on June 1, 2023.  The City has already paid the DDA’s $250,000 annual allocation for Fiscal 
Year 2023.  Therefore, per the stipulations of the MOU, the DDA’s account will reimburse the 
City for these payroll costs in the amount up to $41,432.   
Arts Cheyenne MOU.  Included in the General Fund reserves request is $50,000 as a result of the 
new Arts Cheyenne MOU approved by the Governing Body on February 27, 2023. 
OTHER FUNDS – NEW GRANTS 
The City of Cheyenne has received the following new grants.  They are as follows, by fund: 
Local and State Grants Fund (011) – New Grants Received 
•
$122,470 –  Cheyenne Junior League Complex Multiuse Storage Facility Grant FY23
•
$82,587 – National Opioid Settlement Grants FY23
•
$31,161 – Cheyenne Fire Rescue UTV Equipment Grant FY23
Law Enforcement Grant Fund (024) – New Grants Received 
•
$6,000 – Crisis Intervention Team Training Grant FY23
Federal Grants Fund (025) – New Grants Received 
•
$2,504 – Board and Staff Training: Saving Places Conference Grant FY23

Transit Fund (027) – Increases to Grants Received 
• $110,667 – Purchase of three (3) new 2023 E-350 XL U5X AWD transit vehicles.  These 
new transit vehicles will replace three current transit vehicles that have surpassed their 
useful life and will be sent to auction. 
OTHER FUNDS – NEW REVENUE AND RESERVES REQUESTS 
• $30,000 – Weed and Pest Fund reserves to pay for the installation of a virus testing station 
in the Weed and Pest office. 
• $574,155 – Special Purpose Option Tax 2012 Ballot reserves to pay for additional expenses 
pertaining to the build-out of the third floor of the Cheyenne Public Safety Center. 
• $300,000 –  Recreation Programs Fund reserves request for engineering work pertaining 
to the new gymnastics’ facility. 
• $35,000 –  Recreation Programs Fund reserves request for the purchase of infield mix 
used to level and improve the surface of City ballpark infields. 
• $2,000 - Miscellaneous Agency Funds reserves request to be added to the WARM Training 
funds account for training related expenses. 
• $1,500 - Miscellaneous Agency Funds reserves request to be moved to the Cheyenne Angel 
account for engraving services. 
As always, if you have any questions, please give me a call at 637-6336 or email at 
rlockman@cheyennecity.org.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-04-04_TotalExtraction_83661642.pdf
1 
 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Lonnie Olson, Planner I 
Subject: Resolution for naming the median which contains the Historic Airport Fountain, located to 
the east of the intersection of Central Avenue and East 8th Avenue, to be known as Airport 
Fountain Park  
Date: 
April 10, 2023 
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is the naming of the median which contains the Historic Airport Fountain as 
Airport Fountain Park. The site is a .07 acre +/- parcel located to the east of the intersection of Central 
Avenue and East 8th Avenue. The Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board had a landscape study done in 
2009 as the fountain was being threatened with demolition. Since the initial landscape study, the 
Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board has restored the Historic Airport Fountain over two phases of work 
for a total cost of $132,684.08 completed in 2022.  
 
City Naming Policy:  
 
See attached City of Cheyenne Naming and Memorial Policy, Resolution 6139, dated April 26th, 2021.   
 
Threshold: As a guideline but not a limitation, the threshold for naming rights on parks or 
buildings would include one or preferably more of the following: 
 
1. Deeding to the City most if not all the land on which the park or building to be 
named will be situated, 
2. Payment of one-half or more of the capital costs of constructing a park or building 
to be named (depending on availability of matching funds or grants), 
3. Long-term endowment for the operation, repair and maintenance of the donated 
park or building, 
4. Provision of significant program costs for facilities that will serve City program and 
facility needs. 
 
 
 
 
Procedure: 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

2 
 
An applicant must compile a petition and make presentation to the City to recommend naming or 
renaming a park or recreational facility.  Applicants shall ensure that the request meets the criteria for 
proposed names as presented in the City Naming and Memorial Policy: 
 
a. The applicant has provided a written notification to the City Clerk’s office, stating reasons 
for the naming request. 
b. The applicant has provided a petition containing 307 certified signatures, including name, 
address, and phone numbers of city residents.  The petitions were provided to the Clerk’s 
office on March 15, 2023. 
c. A presentation of the naming proposal was made to staff of Community Recreation and 
Events on Tuesday, April 4, 2023. 
d. The resolution is supported and sponsored by Councilman Pete Laybourn. 
 
Enclosures: 
Resolution 
Map 
City Clerk Certified Naming Petition 
Written Notification to the City Clerk’s Office 
City of Cheyenne Naming and Memorial Policy 
 
 
 

 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITILED:  "A RESOLUTION FOR NAMING THE MEDIAN WHICH CONTAINS 
THE HISTORIC AIRPORT FOUNTAIN, LOCATED TO THE EAST OF 
THE INTERSECTION OF CENTRAL AVENUE AND EAST 8TH AVENUE, 
TO BE KNOWN AS AIRPORT FOUNTAIN PARK." 
 
WHEREAS; the Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board is created by the Cheyenne 
Historic Preservation Ordinance, City of Cheyenne Municipal Code, Section 2.64.010, et seq; 
 
WHEREAS; Preserving the historic integrity of Cheyenne, Wyoming is the Cheyenne 
Historic Preservation Board’s mission; 
 
WHEREAS; the Historic Cheyenne Airport Fountain was built in 1935 to memorialize the 
heritage of aviation in Cheyenne;  
 
WHEREAS; the Historic Cheyenne Airport Fountain was added to the National Register 
of Historic Places in 1985; 
 
WHEREAS; the Historic Cheyenne Airport Fountain was neglected for many years and 
had its mechanical system turned off for decades; 
 
WHEREAS; the Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board in 2009 initiated a landscape study 
on the area surrounding the Historic Airport Fountain due to the threat of demolition; 
 
WHEREAS; the Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board has been awarded grants, received 
donations, and held fundraisers to have a complete restoration conducted on the Historic Airport 
Fountain for a total cost of $132,684.08 over two phases of work completed in 2022; and, 
 
WHEREAS; the median containing the Historic Airport Fountain does not have a name.  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the median that contains the Historic Airport Fountain located 
east of the intersection of Central Avenue and East 8th Avenue be known as "Airport Fountain 
Park" in recognition of the efforts of the Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board in restoring and 
preserving the historical integrity of Cheyenne. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that this resolution shall be 
effective upon passage and execution by the Mayor and City Clerk. 
 
 
 
9:45 am, Apr 05 2023

RESOLUTION NO.
6139
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION ADOPTING THE CITY OF CHEYENNE’S STATEMENT OF
DESIGN STANDARDS, NAMING, MEMORIAL AND GIFTING POLICY.”
WHEREAS, the City Cheyenne desires to adopt the City of Cheyenne Design Standards
for a Naming, Memorial, and Gifting Policy; and
WHEREAS, the City has received requests by members of the Governing Body for such
standards and policies; and
WHEREAS, a standardized policy will improve the efficiency and contiguity for design
standards, naming, memorials and giftings for the city as a whole.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the City of Cheyenne’s Design Standards Naming and
Memorial, and Gifting Policy, which is attached, is hereby adopted and made effective June lS,
2021
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this 2cth day of
April
, 2021
Patrick Collins, Mayor
(SEAL)
ATTEST:
Knstina F. Jones, Cic(lerk

CITY OF CHEYENNE
2101 O’NEIL AVENUE, CHEYENNE, WYOMING, 82001
www.cheyenncity.org
City of Cheyenne
Naming and Memorial Policy
It is the policy of the City of Cheyenne (City) that the naming of new and renaming of existing
facilities be reserved for exceptional circumstances and that the naming process comply with the
guidelines and procedures set forth in this policy.
A. Purpose:
These policies and procedures are intended to guide a) any individual or community group that is
interested in having a park, building, or major feature named for a significant person, event, or
place, b) any individual, group, or business that is interested in having their significant donation
(park, building, major feature) named, and c) decision makers.
B. Definitions:
1.
Parks. All traditional designed parks, natural open spaces, historic sites, golf courses,
specialized parks (e.g. Botanic Gardens, Skate Park), and trails under the department’s
jurisdiction or management
2.
Buildings. Significant structures that house City functions and programs
3.
Major Features. Major, permanent components of City facilities, (e.g. ball fields,
swimming pools, tennis courts, playgrounds, amphitheaters, fountains, artwork or
physical features lakes). Rooms within Buildings are considered to be Major Features.
4.
Amenities. Smaller furnishings and facilities (e.g. Benches, small fountains, tables, etc.)
Amenities are not formally named. Recognition for donated amenities is covered under
the Gift and Memorial Policy.
C. Intent: Naming or renaming City facilities is often complex as naming is a powerful and
permanent identity for a public place. Excessive naming of individual features and
facilities can be confusing to the public.
Approval of naming requests is a prestigious, cautious process that typically involves
meeting with the appropriate City Department, petition process and verification, public
meetings held in conjunction with governing body meetings, and resolution approval.
The policy of the City is to reserve the naming or renaming of facilities to those
circumstances which tradition and practice have shown to best serve the interests of the
City and assure a worthy and enduring legacy for the City. To this end, the City supports
consideration of naming requests in the following three broad categories:
1
P

1.
Exceptional Individuals
- recognition of an exceptional City leader or a dedicated
supporter of the City
2.
Historic Events, Places, and Persons
— recognition of a major historical event or place
or person(s), local landmarks, prominent geographical locations of historic, social and
cultural significance
3.
Major Gifts
- recognition of community generosity in the form of gifts of time, skills,
resources and products, and money donated by public and private foundations,
individuals, businesses
D. General Principles. In considering any proposal to name or rename a Park, Building, or
Major Feature, the following will be considered:
1.
Lasting historical, cultural, and social significance
2.
Name engenders a strong and positive image
3.
Name memorializes or commemorates people, places, or events of enduring importance
to the community or the nation
4.
Name engenders significant ties of friendship and mutual recognition and support within
the community and/or with those outside of the community
5.
Name is identified with some major achievement or the advancement of the public good
within the community or the nation
6.
Name is particularly suitable for the park, building, or major feature based on the
location, history, or surrounding neighborhood
7.
Name has symbolic value that transcends its ordinary meaning or use and enhances the
character and identity of the park, building, or major feature
8.
Ensure any naming request that accompanies a corporate gift will not result in undue
commercialization
E Renaming Parks, Buildings, and Major Features. Proposals to rename facilities are not
encouraged and should be entertained only after fully investigating and considering the potential
impact of dropping the current name. Names that have become ingrained or widely accepted in
the community should not be abandoned unless there are compelling reasons and strong public
sentiment for doing so.
Historical or commonly-used place names should be preserved wherever possible.
F. Naming or Renaming for Exceptional Individuals. The following guidelines apply to
Raining or renaming requests that result from either a community process or major gift.
Priority for naming facilities after deceased persons should be given in the following order to
those who have significant and lasting contributions:
•
to the City of Cheyenne; or
•
to the nation.
Naming Parks and Buildings after national or international figures should be rare and only upon
a substantial demonstration of the figure’s connection to or special importance in the Cheyenne
community or the State of Wyoming.
2 I

Naming of facilities after people or a group of people who perish in or survive a tragic event or
war should be considered only well after the public shock generated by the tragic event or war
has lessened.
Potential sites for such memorials should be focused on facilities that are more known for their
serene and contemplative nature rather than active recreational locations, such as playing fields
and recreation centers.
Emphasis should be placed on the contributions or heroic actions of these people during their
lifetime, rather than the circumstances of their death or survivorship.
G. Naming and Renaming for Historic Events, Places, and Persons. When a facility is
located near or otherwise associated with events, places, and people of historic, cultural, or social
significance, it is appropriate to consider naming such facilities after such events, places, and
people. The relationship of the facility to the events, places, and people of historic, cultural, or
social significance should be demonstrated through research and documentation. Such naming
will follow the public input and Council approval process.
H. Naming and Renaming for Major Gifts. Typically, the donation of gifts to the City of
Cheyenne should be reward in itself, with recognition being given as set forth in the Gift Policy.
Occasionally, a gift will be made to the City that is of such magnitude and generosity that
naming of such new park or facility in honor of or at the request of the benefactor will be
considered. Any request to rename an existing park or facility that is associated with a major gift
to expand or refurbish that park or facility must meet the naming guidelines but is discouraged.
I. Threshold. As a guideline but not a limitation, the threshold for naming rights on parks or
buildings would include one or preferably more of the following:
1.
deeding to the City most if not all of the land on which the park or building to be named
will be situated
2.
payment of one-half or more of the capital costs of constructing a park or building to be
named (depending on the availability of matching funds or grants)
3.
long-term endowment for the operation, repair and maintenance of the donated park or
building,
4.
provision of significant program costs for facilities that will serve City program and
facility needs
Likewise, as a guideline but not a limitation, the threshold for naming rights on major features
would include one or preferably more of the following:
I.
payment of the capital costs for constructing and installing a major feature
2.
some long-term endowment for the operation, repair and maintenance of the donated
major feature
3.
provision of significant program costs for any major feature giving rise to or supporting a
City program or operation
J. Other Requirements. In no case shall naming or renaming be considered unless the major
gift is deemed acceptable in accordance with the Gift Policy and allowable under the provisions
of City governance.
3 I P
2. c

K. Naming for Persons. Benefactors seeking naming rights for major gifts will be encouraged
to follow the guidelines herein. An exception will be considered on its own merits. A major
feature that has been donated or refurbished may be named for a living person(s) provided that
said person(s) is of good reputation.
Whatever contract accompanies the gift and naming rights should address all conditions applying
to the naming, including time limits for naming of features. However, in addition, the City
reserves the right to rename any park, building, or major feature if the person for whom it is
named turns out to be disreputable or subsequently acts in a disreputable way.
L. Naming for Entities. A corporation, association, and other legally created entity making a
major gift may request that the name of that entity be associated with the name of the park,
building, or major feature which is the subject of the major gift. Each request is evaluated on its
own merits by the City.
In making a recommendation for a corporate name, the reputation of the company and
community support should be considerations. Corporate logos, brands, insignias, may be used as
part of any name association or naming. No direct advertising is allowed. Renaming of any
existing park or building is discouraged even when associated with a major enhancement gift.
The City reserves the right to remove an entity name association on a park or building or to
rename a major feature if the entity turns out to be disreputable or subsequently acts in a
disreputable way.
M. Other Considerations.
Typically, a park should not be subdivided for the purposes of naming unless there are readily-
identifiable physical divisions (major roads, waterways, etc.) in the park and other compelling
reasons for having more than one name for a park. This limitation on naming a park should not
p1-event giving a different name for a trail, building, or major feature located in or near the park.
However, care should be taken in giving a name to a trail, building, or major feature that is
different from the name of the park so that confusion is not caused for the users of the differently
named facilities.
Facilities that are held by the City through a short-term lease or use agreement or improvements
that have a limited life span or occupancy should not be named.
All signs on parks and buildings must meet the City’s or responsible Department’s graphic and
signage standards. No specialized signage will be displayed.
If a new park or building is completed and no suggestion for a name has come from the
community or in association with a gift, the City will recommend a name, using any organized
public process (i.e. nominations, contest, etc.) that they decide upon.
N. Procedures for Community or Citizen Requests to Name or Rename a Park
or Building.
1.
Applicant’s Process. An applicant must compile a petition and make a presentation to
the City to recommend naming or renaming a park or recreational facility. Applicants
shall ensure that the request meets the criteria for proposed names as presented in this
policy. Details of the process include:
41 P

a.
Written notification to the City Clerk’s office to start the process. It should
include the reasons for the request. The reasons will be included in the petition
language and template prepared by the Clerk’s Office. The petitions can be picked
up, mailed, or emailed to:
City of Cheyenne City Clerk
2101 O’VeilAvenue
Cheyenne, WY 82001
A formal filing of the petitions with the Clerk at the above address as soon as
necessary signatures have been gathered. The following criteria for the petition
must be met:
b.
The petition shall state the reasons for the proposed name.
c.
The petition should demonstrate community support for the proposed name.
d.
The petition should contain a description and/or map depiction of the boundaries
of the Park or Building to be named or renamed.
e.
The petition may only be signed by residents of the City of Cheyenne.
f.
The petition should show the name, address and telephone number of each signer.
g.
The number of signatures required should be a minimum of 300 for small
neighborhood parks and their Major Features; 500 for community parks
(generally over 20 acres) and their Major Features; and 1,000 for regional parks
(over 50 acres or regional draw) and their Major Features. Major buildings must
have a minimum of 500 signatures.
h.
A formal presentation of the naming or renaming proposal shall be made to the
Department.
i.
Within 45 days after the completed petitions are filed and formal presentation, the
Department will make a determination about the proposed name.
j.
The applicant shall have a supportive and sponsoring Council member for the
Naming Resolution. The applicant is advised to track the Naming Resolution
through the process.
k.
The applicant should attend the City Council and committee meetings and be
prepared to speak briefly about the request. Other interested persons can be
invited to attend as well. The applicant should ensure that the sponsoring Council
member can attend the meetings.
0. Procedures for Community Requests to Name or Rename Major Features
The same petition and procedures for Naming Parks and Buildings apply to Major Features,
except City Council action is not required.
P. Procedures for the Naming and Renaming of Parks, Buildings, and Major
Features Associated with Major Gifts
1.
The responsible Department Director overseeing the park, building or major feature will
submit a proposal for the naming or renaming of any park, building, or major feature that
is associated with a major gift. The petition process is not necessary.
5 I P

2.
For naming or renaming a park or a building, City Council approval through a Naming
Resolution is required (as provided above) following a favorable recommendation by the
Department. A Public Hearing is strongly recommended.
3.
For naming or renaming major features, only responsible Department approval is
required.
6 I P

City of Cheyenne
Gift Policy
It is the policy of City of Cheyenne (City) to actively encourage gifts that reinforce its mission
and core services. Acceptance and recognition of gifts (property, goods or cash) must comply
with the guidelines and procedures set forth in this policy.
A. Purpose:
This policy is intended to help potential donors understand how the City decides whether to
accept a proposed gift and how that donor can be recognized and thanked; to guide decisions of
the City, elected officials, and staff.
The City has benefited from a rich legacy of community generosity. Over the decades Cheyenne
residents and businesses have given gifts of their time, materials, products, and money. Public
and private foundations have invested in the City. A donation can dramatically increase the
quality of a program, park, or building or make the difference during economically lean times.
The City encourages and facilitates public and private gifts, bequests, and such contributions that
enhance and support services, parks, buildings, programs, and goals of equity. The City wants to
assure donors that their gift will be well-maintained and recognized. These policies outline the
criteria for accepting gifts and recognizing donors.
B. DefinItIons
1. GIft A gift is a freely given donation of property, goods, or cash to the City, preferably
with no expectation of return. If the gift is contingent upon a special request or
restriction, the gift is considered as being subject to a “condition”. Property can range
from land and buildings to smaller goods. Cash could include actual cash, discounts, or
investments. Gifts may be donated for a specific purpose or may be general in nature.
2. NamIng Benefits. Naming is the permanent name associated with a park, building, or
major features within a— or building and is an exceptional way in which to recognize
a substantial gift. See the Naming & Memodal Policy.
3.
Corporate SponsorshIp. Corporate sponsorship differs from gifts; it is financial or
inkmnd support from a for-profit entity for a specific City event, project, program and site
in exchange for tangible and intangible marketing benefits to both the sponsor and the
City. See the Corporate Sponsorship Policy.
4. General PrIncIples: The following principles are intended to help potential donors
understand the process and complexity for the City in accepting gifts.
a) The City works closely with each donor to provide meaningful recognition. The
fmal decision is made by the City and is guided by established recognition and
signage guidelines and applicable policies. A variety of methods providing gift
71 Page

recognition is encouraged and naming benefits are reserved for the most
compelling contributors to the City or major donors.
C. Considerations
1.
The City must have adequate operating budget and staffing to accept, implement, and
maintain the gift. Consequently, gifts that require levels of service, maintenance, or
staffing beyond current levels shall consider necessary funding for the operating budget.
2.
Gifts are encouraged that help eliminate any disparity in the quality of facilities and
services across the City.
3.
To promote departmental priorities and the goal of equity, the City and its partners will
proactively offer potential donors a variety of meaningful and relevant gift programs or
ideas, such as tribute walls, bench programs, donor plaques and work to ensure wide
geographic distribution.
4.
Gifts must be given with the understanding that:
a.
they become the property of the City and are subject to the laws, policies and
procedures that govern the City and the department.
b.
the department is not obligated to replace the gift if it is stolen, vandalized, worn
out, irreparably damaged or destroyed or, in the case of plant material, dies.
c.
gifts must bear no emblem of, or reference to, firearms, tobacco, alcohol, or
sexually explicit materials.
5.
Once accepted, the City reserves the following rights with respect to the gift:
a.
to remove or return any donated item
b.
to determine where the improvement will be located and when it will be installed
(unless a specific location is agreed upon in advance)
c.
to determine the life span of the improvement and to remove it when appropriate
d.
to determine style and design of the improvement to fit City specifications,
historic and environmental context
e.
to require the groups or individuals involved to meet City standards of liability
and insurance coverage if volunteer labor is used
D. Guidelines for Accepting Gifts
1.
The City welcomes these generous gifts as an opportunity to enhance services as long as
they are consistent with City and departmental policies and regulations; respect the
physical beauty of public spaces; and reaffirm the City’s mission and core services. In
examining any proposed gifts, contemplate if the project could be built or happen
otherwise, and consider the following individually and collectively:
a.
Gift is consistent with the mission, values, priorities, and master plans of the City
b.
Gift has community or neighborhood support
c.
Gift meets departmental specifications, such as construction quality
d.
Gift enhances existing programs and facilities
e.
Gift reinforces the City’s priorities
f.
Gift addresses the Department’s goal of equity of services and facilities across the
City
8 I P

g.
Adequate budget and staffing to maintain the gift
2.
Conditional gifts may include requests for naming parks, recreation facilities, or
buildings (see Naming and Memorial Policy for details on naming) or requests for
permitting priority or access to facilities built or improved as a gift. When considering
requests for permitting priority or access, ensure that the request does not:
a.
negatively impact public access in a way that exceeds the value of the gift or the
public good served by the gift
b.
negatively impact equity of services across the City
c.
have the effect of turning a public facility into essentially a private one
d.
negatively impact existing programs, activities, and uses
3.
Other gifts subject to conditions will be considered on a case-by-case basis. These types
of gifts include but are not limited to the following:
a.
Any monetary gift that is time limited or requires subsequent donor approval of
project plans
b.
Any monetary gift that is restricted to a specific use
c.
A real property gift subject to defensible fee, reversionary interest, or life estate
d.
Any gift that requires or restricts the public use or access to the gift in a manner not in
conformance with federal or state law, City Charter or Ordinances, or department
policies and rules and regulations
e.
Any gift that requires action by the City and/or department
f.
Any gift that requires a substantial financial commitment or a “maintenance effort”
by the City or department as a prerequisite for or in association with a gift
E. Procedures for Accepting a Gift
1.
The City may approve or decline any proposed gift. Any gift of real or personal property
must be approved by the affected Department Director and be supported by the
governmental process.
2.
Any proposed major gift that substantially impacts the public use of parks or building,
impacts the City’s resources, or has an accompanying condition requested with it must be
approved by the City. If a gift includes the request to name a public park, building, or
major feature, it must follow the Naming and Memorials Policy.
3.
The process for donating a gift to the City is to:
a.
Contact the City Clerk directly about any proposed gift (except for trees)
City Clerk
2101 O’Neil Avenue
Cheyenne, WY 82001
b.
A City staff member will work directly with the donor for the appropriate reviews
and recognition for each gift.
F. Procedures and Guidelines for Recognition of a Gift
9 I P

1.
The City wants to recognize the generosity of its donors in a meaningful way. At the
same time, the City strives to minimize visual impacts in parks, buildings, and major
features. The staff will work with the donor to select a variety of options to thank and
recognize them for their gift. That can include recognition in marketing materials, City
website, collective gift walls, or small plaques. The following guidelines apply to all gift
recognition:
a.
All forms of recognition must meet City design and maintenance guidelines.
b.
The City will establish and follow guidelines for the size, material, color and
location of a plaque or other name recognition media.
c.
Often many people may be “behind the scenes contributors” to a major gift (e.g.,
elected officials, staff members, or community leaders). However, permanent
recognition, by name, should be limited to the donor(s).
d.
Permanent recognition of donors in public spaces and parks must have minimal
visual impact, be tasteful, and appropriate to the scale of the place and gift. Plaques
will be directly connected to the gift or in a collective display area.
e.
Permanent recognition in a park or building can include the name of individuals,
organizations, and corporations. Product names and company logos are considered
advertising and are not acceptable. If a gift is for a series or group of improvements
(e.g., new signs, trail markers, play equipment components) recognition will be
limited to one primary location.
f.
Other visible recognition objects will be limited to areas recognized as “built”
environments (e.g. benches, picnic tables, buildings, play areas, ball fields, tennis
courts).
g.
Overall guidelines for accepting gifts and naming requests are covered in City
policies: Gift Policy, Corporate Sponsorship Policy, and Naming and Memorial
Policy.
2.
Procedures and Guidelines for Permanent Recognition. These are guidelines for
permanent, public recognition of gifts. The City will also recognize donors and gifts in a
variety of ways such as printed materials and announcements.
G. Internal Department Process anti Materials for Recognition of Gifts
1.
Use Simple wording:
“Dedicated to.... “ “In honor of
“ “Iii celebration of...”
2.
No logos
3.
Date of dedication or installation (no birthldeath dates)
4.
City should have a master plan of general areas where benches could be added and other
material gifts (shelters, equipment, plazas, playgrounds, flower beds, etc.)
5.
Recognition for multiple gifts (e.g., a series of trail markers, many signs, all the
equipment) will be consolidated into one primary place. Recognition attached to gift if
possible.
6.
Kept in scale to not detract from the gift or context
7.
Connected to an architectural element (in the wall, pavement, boulder)
10 I P
.

8.
Details (materials, font, graphics determined by City)
9.
Logos: no company logos or brands incorporated into permanent recognition on display
in a park or recreation facility
10. Signs
1 1. Standard City design preferable for large gifts (naming rights). Smaller gifts will be
recognized by plaques, interpretive materials, or other appropriate recognition. No logos.
11
P
e

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING SUBMISSION OF AN APPLICATION 
TO THE WYOMING DEPARTMENT OF TRANSPORTATION FOR A 
WYOMING RURAL PUBLIC TRANSPORTATION PROGRAM GRANT 
TO PROVIDE THE LOCAL MATCH FOR OPERATING FUNDS FOR THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE FOR ITS TRANSIT PROGRAM." 
 
 
WHEREAS, the United States Department of Transportation is authorized to make grants 
to states through the Federal Transit Administration (FTA) to support operating, project 
administration and maintenance assistance projects for non-urbanized area public transportation 
systems under 49 U.S.C. § 5311 of the Bipartisan Infrastructure Law (BIL), Public Law No. 117-
58, November 15, 2021, as amended ("Section 5311"); 
 
WHEREAS, the Wyoming Department of Transportation (WYDOT) has been designated 
by Governor Gordan to administer Section 5311; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne, acting through the Cheyenne Transit Program, wishes 
to apply for financial assistance; and, 
 
 
WHEREAS, the contract for financial assistance will impose certain obligations upon the 
applicant, including possible provisions for the applicant to provide the local share of project costs. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE: 
 
1. 
That the Mayor is authorized to execute and file an application for financial 
assistance on behalf of the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Transit Program, 
with the WYDOT to aid the financing of operating, project administration and 
maintenance assistance for projects pursuant to Section 5311; 
 
2. 
That the Mayor is authorized to furnish such additional information as the WYDOT 
may require in connection with the application of the project; and, 
 
3. 
That the Mayor is authorized to execute grant and cooperative agreements with the 
WYDOT on behalf of the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Transit Program. 
 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that Mayor Patrick Collins, Mayor is designated as the 
authorized representative of the City of Cheyenne to act on behalf of the Governing Body on all 
matters relating to this grant application.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-04-04_TotalExtraction_16457257.pdf
 
1
RESOLUTION NO. _____________ 
 
ENTITLED:  "A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING SUBMISSION OF AN APPLICATION 
TO THE WYOMING WATER DEVELOPMENT COMMISSION GROUND 
WATER EXPLORATORY GRANTS PROGRAM FOR THE BORIE 
WELLFIELD EXPANSION PROJECT IN THE AMOUNT OF $400,000.00 
AND AUTHORIZING THE DIRECTOR OF THE BOARD OF PUBLIC 
UTILITIES TO EXECUTE THE APPLICATION AND ALL RELATED 
DOCUMENTS." 
 
WHEREAS, Wyoming Statute § 15-7-401, provides for a Board of Public Utilities 
(BOPU) to manage, operate, maintain, and control municipal waterworks, sanitary sewer system 
and sewage disposal plants; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Cheyenne City Code, Section 2.68.010, establishes that the BOPU has the 
exclusive authority to manage, operate, maintain and control the Cheyenne Municipal 
Waterworks, Sanitary Sewage System and Sewage Disposal Plants of the City and to make all 
rules and regulations necessary for the safe, economical and efficient operation and maintenance 
of the above facilities; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the 2013 Water and Wastewater Master Plan and 2005 Cheyenne Belvoir 
Ranch Groundwater, Level II, Study recommend the development of additional groundwater 
resources; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne and BOPU own groundwater resources in the High 
Plains Aquifer under the eastern portion of Belvoir Ranch (Borie Wellfield); 
 
WHEREAS, the BOPU desires to pursue the Borie Wellfield Expansion Project 
(PROJECT) to locate, drill, complete, and test two new wells; 
 
WHEREAS, the PROJECT cost estimate for engineering, construction, and other 
administrative costs is eight hundred thousand dollars ($800,000), which includes drilling services, 
construction and miscellaneous costs; 
 
WHEREAS, the PROJECT meets the criteria and is eligible for consideration of grant 
funding from the Wyoming Water Development Commission (WWDC) Ground Water 
Exploratory Grants program in the amount of four hundred thousand dollars ($400,000), fifty 
percent (50%); 
 
WHEREAS, the BOPU will match funds in the amount of four hundred thousand dollars 
($400,000), fifty percent (50%), from System Development Fees as part of Fiscal Year 2024 
Budget; and  
 
 
WHEREAS, the BOPU recognizes that the PROJECT is necessary and that it is essential 
to provide additional water supply for the City of Cheyenne, especially during potential Colorado 
River Curtailment. 
10:46 am, Apr 18 2023

 
2
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, that an application be submitted to the Wyoming Water Development 
Commission for a Ground Water Exploratory Grant in the amount of four hundred thousand dollars 
($400,000.00) for the Borie Wellfield Expansion Project; and  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Mayor, the City Clerk, and the Director of the 
Board of Public Utilities are hereby designated as the authorized representatives of the City of 
Cheyenne to act on behalf of the City of Cheyenne in all matters related to this application and 
related documents, including any amendments to the Project Agreement.

WATER DEVELOPMENT PROGRAM APPLICATION 
GROUND WATER EXPLORATORY GRANTS 
Grant funding for ground water studies is based on WWDC recommendations and is sourced from block 
appropriations by the legislature from the Water Development Accounts.  The WWDC authorizes ground water 
grant expenditures as a maximum 75% project cost share to eligible entities before project work can begin.  
Applications to fund new or continuing groundwater studies must be submitted at least 45 days prior to scheduled 
WWDC meetings for consideration.  Ground water exploration grants are promulgated by statute (W.S. 41-2-119. 
Groundwater studies), governed by WWDC Rules and Regulations, and managed according to program 
“Guidelines:” http://wwdc.state.wy.us/groundwater_grant/groundwater_grant_projects.html 
 
Applicants seeking ground water exploration grant funding must be an incorporated municipality, water district, 
water & sewer district, or service & improvement district with taxing and/or assessment authority.  Private 
corporations, individuals, and other special districts are not eligible for assistance.  Use of grant funds is limited to 
feasibility study of and/or exploration for the use of ground water for municipal and rural domestic purposes.  The 
WWDC grants for groundwater studies are not to exceed $400,000 for any one study and/or exploration program.  
The sponsor receiving the grant shall provide at least 25% of the cost of the feasibility study and/or exploration 
program from its own funds.  The sponsor is solely responsible for acquiring access, easements, or rights-
of-way necessary for exploration.  All grants terminate 24 months from the date of the award. 
 
Note:  If you are seeking Level II or Level III funding for a ground water source supply project, this is the wrong 
application. You must complete a Planning or Construction program application for Municipal or Rural Domestic 
System projects. 
 
APPLICATION REQUIREMENTS 
 
 
The person signing the application must have authority to commit the entity to a binding contract. 
 
 
A notarized copy of a resolution supporting this application passed by the board or other governing 
body of the entity must be provided. 
 
 
A project area map (8.5” x 11” preferred) showing corporate boundaries, project location and 
features should be provided.  Include any reports or other supporting information available. 
 
 
The project sponsor must govern a system that includes a minimum of 15 taps with meters on each 
tap.

wells, they (and two other existing wells) will be connected to the BOPU Borie Wellfield and

groundwater supply is needed to supplement BOPU water portfolio in the event of Colorado
River Curtailment and a reduction up to 15,000 acre-feet per year of Cheyenne's firm yield
supply (68% of total).

WHEREAS, Advanced Communications Technology Inc is the owner of the property in 
Laramie County, Wyoming, more specifically described as Lot 3, Block 1, North Range Business 
Park, 13th Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming;
WHEREAS, Advanced Communications Technology Inc ("the Applicant") submitted an 
application to the City of Cheyenne and the Board of Public Utilities ("BOPU") for water and/or
sanitary sewer services outside of the corporate limits of the City of Cheyenne for the above-
referenced properties; 
 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Resolution No. 3577, BOPU was instructed to present to the 
Governing Body for its review any application for the provision of water and/or sanitary sewer 
services to any individual or entity outside the corporate limits of the City of Cheyenne;
 
 
WHEREAS, BOPU has reviewed the application submitted by the Applicant, and BOPU 
and the Applicant will approve and execute applicable Outside User Agreement(s) upon approval 
by the Governing Body pursuant to Resolution No. 3577; and
WHEREAS, the Governing Body has reviewed the applications for water and/or sanitary 
sewer services submitted by the Applicant and has determined that approval is in the best interest 
of the City of Cheyenne. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, THAT the application submitted by the Applicant to BOPU for 
water and/or sanitary sewer services outside of the corporate limits of the City of Cheyenne for the 
property described as Lot 3, Block 1, North Range Business Park, 13th Filing, Laramie County, 
Wyoming, is hereby approved.

and execute Outside User Agreement(s).

APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
Resolution 3577 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting connection to BOPU water and sanitary sewer services for property outside City 
limits at an estimated amount of 650 gallons per month. This property is in the North Range Business Park, 
which already has BOPU water and sanitary sewer infrastructure and multiple pre-existing outside user 
agreements.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned in the North Range Business Park PUD in the County.  
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
The request for an outside user agreement has no formal review criteria; however, the following 
elements should be considered when reviewing outside user agreements: 
- 
BOPU capacity 
- 
Needed infrastructure improvements 
- 
Population served 
- 
Considerations on the effect on future annexation(s) 
- 
Fiscal cost/benefit to the City and the BOPU 
- 
Conformance with the Comprehensive Plan 
 
already has infrastructure adjacent to this site. The proposed use of the property is non-residential, and 
the request is largely for personal employee needs. The area has many outside user agreements already 
and the size, shape, and use of this parcel would not prevent or hinder future annexations of either this 
parcel or surrounding land.  
 
Sales tax distribution is based on residents, so this non-residential development would likely only help 
City revenues in that regard. Property tax would increase, but the City would not have any income from 
the property tax in the area. The BOPU may charge a higher rate for outside users and the BOPU

infrastructure is already in place so this agreement would likely be a net benefit to BOPU. City police, 
fire, and sanitation services would have to be extended well outside their current service boundaries to 
service this parcel, so if this parcel were to come into City limits by itself, it would likely result in a net 
fiscal loss; however, if the entire business park were to be annexed at once, the revenue increase would 
likely offset the fiscal impact. 
 
The Comprehensive Plan designates this area as Mixed-use Employment  and the proposed use in this 
location is consistent with that anticipated development pattern.

Conformance with PlanCheyenne is discussed above.    
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, the following comments were received: 
- 
If the building has floor drains then the owner will need to install a sand interceptor. 
- 
If facility provides food for employees, contact Environmental Health (307) 633-4090. 
 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application is acceptable based on the considerations specific to this request 
and therefore recommends approval of the outside user agreement.   
 
The proposed outside user agreement is far outside current city limits, has ready access to BOPU 
to annex the area in the future.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the proposed outside user agreement. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Application 
4. Applicant-submitted proposed site plan

RESOLUTION NO. ________________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION ADOPTING THE CITY OF CHEYENNE'S ANNUAL 
ACTION PLAN 2023, PREPARED BY THE CHEYENNE HOUSING AND 
COMMUNITY DEVELOPMENT OFFICE." 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Department of Housing and Urban Development (HUD) provides 
funding to communities to address housing and urban development problems; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne has been designated by HUD as an Entitlement 
Community, and is eligible to receive an annual allocation of Community Development Block 
Grant (CDBG) funds based on the Comprehensive Consolidated Plan 2020 - 2024 which was 
adopted by the Governing Body on October 12, 2020; and, 
 
 
WHEREAS, the 2023 Annual Action Plan has been developed in compliance with the 
City’s Citizen Participation Plan, which requires citizen input and two public hearings including 
review and recommendations by the Housing and Community Development Office Advisory 
Council.  A copy of the 2023 Annual Action Plan is attached to this resolution. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the 2023 Annual Action Plan is adopted and the 
Governing Body, Mayor, and City Clerk are authorized to submit the Plan, along with 
certificates, and execute all HUD contracts and third-party agreements with operating and 
sponsoring agents necessary to implement and accomplish the 2023 Annual Action Plan. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2023. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor
