EMPLOYEE  ASSISTANCE  PROGRAM  AGREEMENT 
 
This Employee Assistance Program Agreement (AGREEMENT) is made and entered into 
by and between CITY OF CHEYENNE, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(CUSTOMER) and CAREBRIDGE CORPORATION, a Pennsylvania Corporation located at 855 
Springdale Drive, Exton, Pennsylvania (CONTRACTOR). 
Whereas, CONTRACTOR makes available an employee assistance program (“EAP”) in-
cluding work-life assistance services (“WLAS”); and, 
Whereas, CUSTOMER desires to make an EAP available to its employees; and, 
Whereas, CUSTOMER desires to contract with CONTRACTOR to provide an EAP for 
CUSTOMER’s employees and CONTRACTOR desires to provide such EAP to CUSTOMER; 
and, 
Whereas, the Parties desire to enter into this Agreement in order to provide a full statement 
of their respective responsibilities in connection with the provision of an EAP during the term of 
this Agreement. 
NOW THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual promises and covenants contained 
herein, and intending to be legally bound hereby, it is understood and agreed by the parties as 
follows: 
1. DEFINITIONS: 
1.1 COVERED EMPLOYEE shall mean any employee of the CUSTOMER included 
in an eligibility file provided to CONTRACTOR by CUSTOMER. Employees on 
Leave of Absence for FMLA, Short-term Disability, Military Leave and Workers’ 
Compensation are also recognized as COVERED EMPLOYEES if included on the 
CUSTOMER-provided eligibility file. 
1.2 COVERED PERSON shall mean any COVERED EMPLOYEE and any related 
individual residing within the household of the COVERED EMPLOYEE as well 
as children living outside the household to age twenty-six (26). A COVERED 
PERSON may have the option to continue the EAP benefit through COBRA, if the 
option is made available by CUSTOMER. 
1.3 PROVIDER shall mean a third party designated by the CONTRACTOR to provide 
EAP services. 
1.4 COVERED SERVICES shall mean all related employee assistance services ren-
dered to a COVERED PERSON to the extent that such services are for the evalua-
tion of, and counseling for, personal and psychological problems.  The services 
described herein are available, at no cost, to COVERED PERSONS.   
8:12 pm, Apr 18 2023

 
 
 
2
1.5 CISD shall mean critical incident stress debriefing. A debriefing is a proactive in-
tervention involving a group meeting or discussion about a particularly distressing 
critical incident. It is designed to mitigate the impact of a critical incident and assist 
in recovery from the stress associated with the event. CISD services are ideally 
conducted between 24 and 72 hours after the incident. 
1.5.1 Standard Response: Any next day response that is not considered 
immediate or same day. 
1.5.2 Same Day Response: Requests that are over three hours and less 
than 24 hours from scheduled response. 
1.5.3 Immediate Response: Requests that are within three hours of sched-
uled response. Response scheduled after 11pm or before 9am the 
next day in the service area time zone. 
1.6 OCCURRENCE shall mean the presentation of a specific set of problem issues. 
2. CONTRACTOR RESPONSIBILITIES: 
2.1 Maintain a case management system to assess, review and coordinate the delivery 
of COVERED SERVICES by PROVIDERS to COVERED PERSONS seeking 
program services. 
2.2 Maintain a readily accessible network of mental health professionals who will pro-
vide COVERED SERVICES to COVERED PERSONS under this Agreement.  The 
Program will provide up to five (5) consultations per OCCURRENCE and will con-
tinue to provide telephonic coaching throughout the contract term. 
2.3 Assist in the introduction of the EAP for the CUSTOMER and provide supporting 
information for the program.  This support shall include working with 
CUSTOMER’s management and appropriate staff such as persons engaged in hu-
man resources, medical and public relations functions for CUSTOMER.  
2.4 Provide twenty-four-hour, seven day per week, emergency access by phone with 
Counselors available for in-person consultation during normal business hours and 
in the evening and for urgent needs on weekends and holidays. 
2.5 Provide orientation sessions for CUSTOMER management and supervisory per-
sonnel to present the program as a management tool.   
2.6 Provide personal and psychological consultation to COVERED PERSONS through 
face-to-face interviews and/or scheduled phone consultations.  Such assistance will 
be in response to supervisor, management, or self-referrals and will be conducted 
on a basis convenient to the clients involved.  Assistance shall include initial as-
sessment and short-term directive counseling up to a maximum of five (5) consul-
tations per OCCURRENCE.  Any costs incurred as a result of a referral beyond or 
outside of included EAP services will be the responsibility of the individual 
COVERED PERSON and/or their insurance.  Problem assistance shall include 
counseling, referral and information in matters related to: 
2.6.1 Marital and other relationship problems. 
2.6.2 Drug, alcohol and other substance abuse concerns. 

 
 
 
3
2.6.3 Emotional difficulties and mental illness. 
2.6.4 Generalized and work-related stress. 
2.6.5 Child, parent and family concerns. 
2.6.6 Issues of grief and loss. 
2.6.7 Financial and legal pressures/problems. 
2.6.8 Other behavioral and mental health concerns. 
2.7 Where indicated, refer COVERED PERSON to other agencies and individuals for 
assistance.  In making such referrals to other agencies, due regard will be given to 
the appropriateness of the referrals in view of the need, location, cost, health care 
coverage, and available resources; also, COVERED PERSON will be advised that 
they, not the CUSTOMER or CONTRACTOR, shall be responsible for payment of 
all costs associated with assistance rendered as a result of a referral. 
2.8 Make available EAP consultations and/or referrals which can be used for an unlim-
ited number of new clinical issues throughout the plan year.  Reauthorization of 
EAP short-term directive counseling sessions for the same issue can be requested 
by members twelve months from the date of the last EAP session used for that 
particular issue.  The distinction of a case as a “new issue” or the “same issue” will 
be based on the presenting clinical symptoms. 
2.9 Make available legal services to COVERED PERSONS needing to retain an attor-
ney. A referral will be provided and the COVERED PERSON will receive one free 
30-minute consultation with that attorney, in addition to a 25% discount on any 
further services. Referrals are made to local, pre-screened and credentialed attor-
neys. 
2.10 Offering COVERED PERSONS one free 30-minute financial consultation with a 
financial specialist by phone each year. A discount of up to 25% is available if 
additional consultations are desired. 
2.11 Provide employee-focused program features as set forth in ADDENDUM B. 
2.12 CONTRACTOR will make life coaching services available to COVERED 
PERSONS providing up to six (6) telephonic consultation sessions per year. 
2.13 Upon request, consult with the CUSTOMER’s individual managers and/or super-
visors regarding potential referral of an employee to the Program plus ongoing con-
sultation with managers within legally imposed requirements of confidentiality. 
2.14 Provide critical incident response including unlimited telephonic and/or videocon-
ference consultation with a trauma specialist, defusing, and support as required plus 
telephonic outreach and post-incident guidance. Clinically appropriate and neces-
sary onsite and live video CISD’s can be purchased at request of CUSTOMER at 
the following rates: 
2.14.1 Standard CISD’s: $225 per live video or onsite hour plus travel. 
2.14.2 Same Day Response: $270 per live video or onsite hour plus travel. 
2.14.3 Immediate Response: $300 per live video or onsite hour plus travel. 

 
 
 
4
Cancellation Fee: If four hours or less scheduled, fee will be $350. If more than 
four hours scheduled, fee will be $500.   
2.15 Upon request, CONTRACTOR will provide assistance and consultation to 
CUSTOMER in fitness-for-duty cases. CONTRACTOR’s involvement in provid-
ing these services is limited to assisting the CUSTOMER in determining whether a 
fitness-for-duty evaluation by an independent third party might be appropriate and 
providing assistance with finding providers to complete the formal evaluation if 
requested. All costs for fitness-for-duty evaluations and related services will be 
borne by the CUSTOMER or employee based on CUSTOMER’s policies. 
2.16 Provide ongoing professional telephone consultation for work-life needs as set forth 
in ADDENDUM A on a seven (7) day per week, twenty-four (24) hour per basis 
year around. 
2.17 Make available during each year of the term of AGREEMENT twenty-eight (28) 
trainings consisting of sixteen (16) prescheduled online monthly/quarterly cam-
paign web-based e-trainings and twelve (12) prescheduled live webinars selected 
by CONTRACTOR. 
Additional trainings, seminars and fairs (online or onsite) are available from 
CONTRACTOR’s catalog of over sixty (60) developed training topics at the per 
hour rate of $400 plus travel for seminars, $325 for webinars and $80 for fairs.  
Customization of seminars/webinars is available at $150 per design hour. 
2.18 CONTRACTOR to provide the following services regarding substance abuse 
cases: 
2.18.1 Initiate referrals to providers maintaining their Substance Abuse 
Professional (SAP) credential in all cases involving DOT violations. 
2.18.2 Provide identification and coordination of certified SAP’s to 
CUSTOMER. 
2.18.3 Administratively and clinically coordinate care and provide case 
management throughout the SAP evaluations; and 
2.18.4 Provide notification of compliance or non-compliance. 
2.18.5 Provide Department of Transportation (DOT) SAP referrals. Such 
referrals will be provided at a rate of $350 per case. 
2.18.6 All DOT/SAP evaluations with their related costs will be borne by 
CUSTOMER or employee based on CUSTOMER policies to fulfill 
DOT requirements. 
2.18.7 CONTRACTOR has no further administrative, clinical or financial 
responsibility for additional services regarding DOT/SAP referrals. 
2.19 Provide electronic workplace communications to CUSTOMER for distribution and 
including service explanatory videos, informational brochures, wallet cards, web 
site information, posters, and copy for employee newsletters. 
2.20 Provide CUSTOMER, on a quarterly basis, a report on the progress of Employee 
Assistance Program activities including number of persons served, client 

 
 
 
5
demographics, types of cases dealt with, and referrals made.  Reports shall not in-
clude confidential information regarding individual clients served. 
2.21 Represent and warrant that all counseling service referrals will be made to duly 
qualified and licensed mental health professionals, psychologists, social workers, 
and certified employee assistance professionals (CEAPs). 
2.22 Shall comply with applicable federal and state laws concerning preservation of con-
fidentiality of information, including but not limited to HIPAA, relating to 
COVERED PERSONS’ receiving Covered Services pursuant to the terms of this 
Agreement. 
2.23 Maintain general and professional liability insurance as specified in Section 6 of 
this Agreement and provide CUSTOMER proof of same upon request. 
3. CUSTOMER RESPONSIBILITIES: 
3.1 Provide such facilities as may be necessary for planning and evaluation meetings, 
group orientation sessions, and individual conferences with managers, supervisors 
and employees. 
3.2 Assume responsibility for scheduling and notifying its managers, supervisors of 
such meetings. 
3.3 Guide and participate in an active program of communication related to the availa-
bility of the Program to employees, their managers, and supervisors. 
3.4 Assist with internal and external publicity and communications appropriate to es-
tablish and maintain the program. 
3.5 Designate a representative of CUSTOMER to be the liaison/coordinator of the Pro-
gram who shall work cooperatively with the CONTRACTOR and represent the 
CUSTOMER to the CONTRACTOR in the day-to-day contacts regarding services 
covered by this Agreement. 
3.6 Provide an eligibility file of its COVERED EMPLOYEES to CONTRACTOR, up-
dated on a quarterly basis. Eligibility file should not include any PHI or financial 
information. Eligibility file should include only the following: 
3.6.1 Employee Name 
3.6.2 Employee Address 
3.6.3 If applicable: Region/Division and/or Location/Worksite 
3.7 If CONTRACTOR’s performance of its obligations under this agreement is pre-
vented, impeded, or delayed by any act or omission of CUSTOMER or its agents, 
subcontractors, consultants, or employees, CONTRACTOR shall not be deemed in 
breach of its obligations under this agreement or otherwise liable for any costs, 
charges, or losses sustained or incurred by CUSTOMER, in each case, to the extent 
arising directly or indirectly from such prevention, impediment or delay. 
3.8 Provide a copy of the CUSTOMER’s Substance Abuse Policy to the 
CONTRACTOR and ensure that such policy is in accordance with any and all ap-
plicable laws. 

 
 
 
6
4. FEES FOR SERVICES:   
4.1 In consideration of CONTRACTOR’s services to be provided hereunder, during 
the TERM the CUSTOMER shall pay to CONTRACTOR a fee of $1.28 per 
COVERED EMPLOYEE per month, the total amount of such fee shall be as deter-
mined by multiplying the fee specified above by the total number of COVERED 
EMPLOYEES in the CUSTOMER-provided quarterly eligibility file.   
4.2 CONTRACTOR reserves the right to request a renegotiation of the then-effective 
service fee if the annual increase in the Producer Price Index for Selected Health 
Care Industries (Series ID: PCUASHC—ASHC--)  published by the US Depart-
ment of Labor’s Bureau of Labor Statistics for the prior twelve-month period in-
crease by more than 3.0%. 
4.3 Payments of quarterly contractual fees shall be due on the first day of each quarter 
of contract service at the CONTRACTOR’s office, 855 Springdale Drive, Suite 
200, Exton, PA  19341. 
 
5. TERM AND TERMINATION:   
5.1 TERM of CONTRACTOR’s services hereunder shall commence on July 1, 2023 
and shall cover a period of thirty-six (36) months ending June 30, 2026. 
5.2 This Agreement may be terminated by either party upon forty-five (45) days’ prior 
written notice of intent to terminate due to the other party’s committing a material 
breach of the Agreement. In the event the breaching party cures the breach within 
thirty (30) days after receiving such notice, this Agreement shall not terminate. 
5.3 Termination by either party, without cause, may be accomplished by giving written 
notice to the other party of at least ninety (90) days prior to the date of such termi-
nation.  CUSTOMER’s sole obligation shall be to pay CONTRACTOR quarterly 
fees through the ninety (90) days after notification of intent to terminate without 
cause.  Such termination shall constitute a full and final discharge of all obligations 
of CONTRACTOR and CUSTOMER. 
5.4 Suspension of Services: CONTRACTOR reserves the right to suspend or terminate 
access to programs included in Addendum B upon ninety (90) days’ written notice 
if, in CONTRACTOR’s judgment, such services cease to be available on commer-
cially reasonable terms from its third-party vendors. If CONTRACTOR elects to 
suspend or terminate such services, the remaining provisions of this Agreement will 
remain in full force and effect, except that CONTRACTOR will adjust its contrac-
tual fees pro rata to reflect the suspension or termination of the service or services. 
6. INSURANCE AND INDEMNIFICATION: 
6.1 CONTRACTOR agrees to carry and keep in force at all times general liability in-
surance with limits of liability of no less than $1,000,000 per occurrence and 
$3,000,000 aggregate. The CONTRACTOR shall, upon request, provide 
CUSTOMER with a Certificate of Insurance evidencing the required coverage. 
6.2 As allowed by law, each party (INDEMNIFYING PARTY) agrees to defend and 
indemnify the other party, its affiliates and their respective officers, directors and 

 
 
 
7
employees (INDEMNIFIED PARTY) from any claims, losses, damages, liabilities, 
or expenses (including court costs and reasonable attorney’s fees) arising out of or 
resulting from the negligent or willful act or omission or breach of this Agreement 
by INDEMNIFYING PARTY or its officers, directors, employees, or agents, but 
only if INDEMNIFIED PARTY has not, by act or failure to act, materially jeop-
ardized the position of INDEMNIFYING PARTY with respect to the resolution or 
defense of the claim. INDEMNIFIED PARTY must promptly notify 
INDEMNIFYING PARTY upon receipt of notice of any claim or lawsuit and must 
permit INDEMNIFYING  PARTY’S authorized attorneys and personnel to handle 
and control the defense of any such claim or lawsuit. INDEMNIFIED PARTY 
agrees to fully cooperate with and aid in such defense at its own cost. 
INDEMNIFYING PARTY may not, without the prior written consent of

7. MISCELLANEOUS: 
7.1 Independent contractor.  It is expressly acknowledged hereto that the

tended nor shall be construed to create any relationship between the CUSTOMER 
and the CONTRACTOR other than that of independent entities contracting with 
each other solely for the purpose of effecting the provisions of this Agreement or 
to allow CUSTOMER to exercise control or direction over the method or manner 
of the CONTRACTOR 's employees’ performance of this Agreement. 
7.2 Amendment.  This Agreement may not be amended or revised except with the prior 
written consent of the other party. 
7.3 Assignment.  No party may assign its rights or obligations hereunder without the 
prior written consent of the other party. 
7.4 Severability.  Should any part of this Agreement be declared invalid, for any reason, 
the remainder shall continue in full force and be interpreted as if the invalid portion 
were omitted. 
7.5 Force Majeure.  Neither party shall be liable to the other for damages or monetary 
penalties of any kind or deemed in default under this Agreement for any failure to 
perform or delay in performing to the extent that its performance is hindered, de-
layed, or rendered impossible due to an event or occurrence beyond the reasonable 
control of the party, and without its fault or negligence, including, without limita-
tion, the breakdown, malfunction or other failure of any external third party tele-
communication system or other system or mechanism by which information and

          Provision to COVERED PERSONS of Work-Life Services include telephonic assistance referrals,

Addendum B 
 
1. Tess Artificial Intelligence Coaching – Provide 24/7 access to a cCBT-based artificial intelli-
gence coach providing smartphone texting conversations to assist individuals with their emo-
tional concerns.   
2. Thrive Being: Core – Provide access to podcasts as well as a quarterly, on demand learning plan 
from the Being Learning Platform. Learning plans focus on general health and wellbeing. 
3. Thrive Mindfulness: Enhanced – Make available a mindfulness platform featuring programs 
with mindful programs, apps, blogs, and trending articles on mindfulness.  
4. QuitSmart – Provide the QuitSmart Mindfully program which includes up to 10 live 
online sessions, nicotine replacement therapy and other nicotine cessation supports. 
5. Carebridge EAP Smartphone App – Make available a smart phone app offering access to re-
sources, videos, assessments, financial calculators, health and wellness, meditation exercises, 
mindfulness and webinars.  
6. Carebridge Member Website – Make available a member website, providing access to re-
sources to help build resilience and get answers to difficult questions. Resources can also be used 
to supplement and enhance user’s counseling experience.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
April 19, 2023 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and AVI 
Professional Corporation for the Public Works Improvements project 
 ______________________________________________ 
Two (2) Responsive Proposals were submitted under Request for Proposal (RFP) #9-23, for the 
Public Works Improvements Project.  The consultant will prepare design specifications for the 
repair of various concrete and asphalt components at the Transfer Station, Traffic Building, Street 
and Alley Shop, Landfill, Fleet Maintenance Facility, and Municipal Building. 
 
The proposals were received from:  
1. AVI Professional Corporation 
2. Inberg Miller Engineers 
 
Upon review of the two (2) responsive proposals by the evaluation panel, the group has 
recommended acceptance of the proposal submitted by AVI Professional Corporation, for a project 
award amount of $265,290.00. 
 
Basis for award was based on a Qualifications based review and scoring of proposals:  AVI 
Professional Corporation submitted the highest scoring proposal, therefore their sealed pricing 
envelope was opened & negotiated. 
 
Funding:   
020-26-2616-42-21301 (1% Sales Tax 19-22 / Street & Alley - Prof. Services) 
023-14-1417-43-21301 (Solid Waste Fund / Transfer Station - Prof. Services) 
020-26-2617-42-21301 (1% Sales Tax 19-22 / Traffic – Prof. Services) 
023-14-1424-43-21301 (Solid Waste Fund / Landfill - Prof. Services) 
101-14-1415-40-21301 (Fleet Maintenance Fund / Fleet - Prof. Services) 
020-26-2615-40-21601 (1% Sales Tax 19-22 / Other Projects – Maintenance) 
 
Total will be split across all GL’s based on actual design services rendered.

The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the above-
stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the 
Agreement.  It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify 
in writing the city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or 
occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change 
to any insurance coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be 
construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  
The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall 
not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 9-23 
Public Works Improvements Project 
Page 4 of 7 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin, or sex. 
 
10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a 
written statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, 
and work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing 
of any payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities 
that may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing 
in this Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or 
employees to act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any 
obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, 
debts, and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this 
Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from date of execution to June 30, 2024.  The parties 
may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 9-23 
Public Works Improvements Project 
Page 5 of 7 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. 
Upon receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the 
Consultant for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of 
termination. Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, 
the City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of 
default of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default 
to commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear 
its own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary 
for the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the 
Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et 
seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, 
enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of 
governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not 
be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental 
immunity. 
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are 
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited 
to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring 
experts and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in 
progress; to examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this 
Agreement; and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work. 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 9-23 
Public Works Improvements Project 
Page 6 of 7 
 
20. 
OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS AND WORK PRODUCT 
All deliverables by the Consultant, all work product of the Consultant, all notes, 
calculations, memoranda, or any other documentation generated by the Consultant 
pursuant to the scope of this Agreement, shall be the property of the City of Cheyenne 
and shall be provided to the City of Cheyenne immediately upon request. 
 
21. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With 
Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age 
Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, 
and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, 
race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection 
with the performance under this Agreement. 
 
22. 
FORCE MAJEURE. 
The performance of the Agreement by either party shall be subject to force majeure 
including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural disaster, war or threat of 
war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized strikes, governmental 
regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by the World Health 
Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government authority or health 
agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-19, H1N1, or similar 
infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other similar occurrence 
beyond the control of the parties, where any of those factors, circumstances, situations, or 
conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or unreasonably delay the performance 
required by this Agreement. The Agreement may be cancelled by either party, without 
liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or amounts paid shall be 
refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written notice to the other party. 
 
23. 
PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION 
The Consultant shall endorse, as required by law, plans and reports prepared under this 
Agreement, and shall affix thereto his or her seal of professional registration, showing 
that he or she is licensed to practice in the State of Wyoming, if necessary. 
 
24. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement consisting of seven (7) pages, Exhibit B – Price Proposal, consisting of 
five (5) pages, Request for Proposal RFP 9-23, consisting of twenty-eight (28) pages, 
Addendum One, consisting of two (2) pages, and the Consultant’s submitted proposal, 
consisting of twenty-seven (27) pages, contains the entire understanding of the parties. 
There are no other terms or conditions, written or oral, concerning or controlling this 
matter. 
 
 
 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 9-23 
Public Works Improvements Project

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-04-04_TotalExtraction_22417734.pdf
PIPELINE EASEMENT 
 
BE IT KNOWN BE ALL PERSONS PRESENT that City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a 
Municipal Corporation, whose address is 2101 O’Neil Avenue Room 101, Cheyenne, WY 82001, 
for itself and its heirs, administrators, successors, and assigns (collectively “Grantor”), for and in 
consideration of the sum of One Hundred and no/100ths Dollars ($100.00) and other good and 
valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency of which is hereby acknowledged and 
confessed, has granted, conveyed, sold, and warranted, and does hereby grant, convey, sell, and 
warrant unto Samson Exploration, LLC, a Texas limited liability company whose address is 110 
West 7th Street, Suite 2000, Tulsa, Oklahoma 74119 and its affiliates, successors, and assigns 
(collectively “Grantee”) an easement, servitude, license, and right to use (the “Easement”) in, 
upon, across, through, over, and under the lands described on the attached Exhibit “A” located in 
Laramie County, Wyoming (the “Lands”), as said Easement is more fully described as follows: 
 
 
Pipeline — The right to use a thirty feet (30’) wide strip across the Lands to survey, lay, 
construct, install, operate, inspect, protect, alter, maintain, improve, repair, change the size of, 
relocate, add, replace, remove, and/or abandon in place one or more pipelines and all valves, 
fittings, devices for controlling electrolysis and/or cleaning pipeline interiors, and/or other 
necessary appurtenances above and below ground, including suitable markers to mark the location 
of the pipeline(s), for the purposes of transportation of oil, gas, petroleum products, water and/or 
any other liquids, gases, or substances which can be transported through pipelines.  This right shall 
be expanded from time to time during any period(s) of construction, maintenance, or repair to a 
width of sixty feet (60’) for so long as such use is reasonably necessary for the operations being 
conducted, and shall revert to the permanent width set forth above upon completion 
thereof.  Grantee shall maintain the uppermost part of the underground portions of said pipelines 
at least thirty-six inches (36”) below the surface of the ground, but this limitation shall not apply 
to any portion of the pipelines or other equipment installed above the surface. 
 
The approximate location(s) of the Easement associated with the pipeline are set out on the 
plat attached hereto and marked as Exhibit “B”.  Grantee may, in its sole judgment, replace the 
preliminary plat attached hereto as Exhibit  “B” with a final or as-built surveyed plat depicting the 
actual boundaries or centerlines of said pipeline by recording a designation of easement location 
(“Designation”) in the county in which the applicable Lands are situated, and any such Designation 
and any exhibits thereto shall supersede and/or replace that portion of the Exhibit “B” attached 
hereto which has been re-surveyed and re-platted as provided herein.  Upon request, Grantee shall 
forward a copy of such Designation to Grantor. 
 
The rights granted herein include the right of ingress and egress over and across any 
existing or future trails, roads, or canals on the Lands which were not constructed by Grantee to 
carry out and fulfill all purposes described herein.  Grantee acknowledges that Grantor intends to 
install a gravel trail on the Lands, and that said trail may at some time become a paved trail.  
Grantee shall repair any damages to such trails, roads, or canals caused by Grantee’s use to as close 
to their condition prior to Grantee’s use as is practicable. 
 
9:29 am, Apr 19 2023

 
The rights granted herein include the right to construct, install, operate, maintain, repair, 
replace, add to, and remove electric, communication, and/or control lines either above or below 
the Lands as may be beneficial to Grantee’s operations.  
 
Without prior, written consent of the Grantee, Grantor shall not construct or permit 
construction within the boundaries of the Easement reflected on Exhibit “B” or a Designation, and 
Grantee shall have the right to prevent the construction therein and the right to remove therefrom, 
any and all types and sizes of houses, barns, buildings, structures, permanent impoundments of 
water, and natural or man-made obstructions, including but not limited to trees, brush, roots, and 
other growth. Grantor shall not, nor permit third parties to, change the grade of the land or remove 
the cover over the Pipeline or excavate on or near such portions of the Easement without prior 
written consent of the Grantee, which consent shall not be unreasonably withheld provided same 
does not present an operational or safety issue for Grantee. 
 
Except as otherwise set forth herein, the consideration paid hereunder includes payment 
for all damages to the Lands, and Grantor hereby acknowledges receipt and sufficiency of said 
payment as full and complete settlement for and as a release of all claims for loss, damage, or 
injury to the Lands arising out of the operations contemplated hereunder.  Grantee shall have the 
right from time to time to cut or clear trees, brush, and other obstructions on the Lands that might 
interfere with the operation, access to, or maintenance of the easements granted herein or any 
facilities or equipment thereon relating to the rights granted herein.  This Easement shall in no way 
diminish, lessen, or remove any rights now or hereafter held by Grantee, its successors or assigns, 
by and through any and all oil, gas, and mineral lease(s) or any other agreements that cover all or 
any portion of the Lands subject to this Easement. 
 
Grantor represents and warrants title to the herein granted Easement unto Grantee, its 
successors and assigns, and does hereby agree to forever defend all and singular such interest unto 
Grantee, its successors and assigns, against any person whomsoever claiming or to claim the 
same.  This Easement and all of the terms, provisions and obligations hereof shall be covenants 
running with the Lands and shall inure to the benefit of and be binding upon Grantor and Grantee 
and their respective successors, heirs, and assigns. 
 
Upon the termination of this Easement, Grantee shall perform all work necessary to purge 
or remove all flammable or explosive substances contained within and to cap or seal any pipelines 
buried pursuant to this Easement in accordance with all applicable laws, regulations, and sound 
engineering practices.  All pipe left in place shall then become and remain the sole property of 
Grantor to the exclusion of any claim by Grantee or any party claiming by, through, or under 
Grantee. At such time Grantor shall accept, and shall be deemed to have accepted, all such 
pipelines “AS-IS” and “WHERE-IS”, with all faults and defects, and without warranty of any kind 
whatsoever, and assumes and agrees to bear all risks and responsibilities related thereto. 
 
GRANTEE SHALL INDEMNIFY GRANTOR AGAINST ANY CLAIMS, DAMAGES, 
DEMANDS, LIABILITIES, AND COSTS TO THE EXTENT ARISING FROM OR RELATED 
TO THE NEGLIGENCE OR MISCONDUCT OF GRANTEE OR ITS EMPLOYEES, AGENTS, 
CONTRACTORS, OR INVITEES IN THE COURSE OF THEIR EXERCISE OF RIGHTS 

 
GRANTED BY THIS EASEMENT, BUT NOT TO THE EXTENT CAUSED BY GRANTOR, 
OR ITS EMPLOYEES, AGENTS, CONTRACTORS, OR INVITEES. 
 
Grantor hereby enters into this Agreement and receives full consideration on behalf of 
Grantor and any surface tenants, surface lessees, permitees, and licensees on the Lands, and their 
respective successors, heirs, and assigns. Grantor is responsible for sharing such consideration 
with any such individual(s) or entity(ies), to the extent they are entitled to compensation related to 
Grantee’s activities. 
 
The rights granted herein are non-exclusive, and Grantor reserves and retains the right to 
convey similar rights to such other persons as Grantor may deem proper; provided, however, that 
all such conveyances shall be subject to Grantee’s rights, and Grantee shall not be unreasonably 
disturbed in the use and enjoyment of the rights granted herein. 
 
In the event Grantee shall be in default or breach of any of the terms of this Easement, 
Grantor shall give written notice to Grantee of such default or breach.  Grantee shall then have 
ninety (90) days within which to commence to remedy any alleged default.   
 
Any notice provided or permitted to be given in this instrument must be in writing and may 
be given by depositing the notice in the United States mail, postage prepaid, certified with return 
receipt requested, and addressed to the party to be notified.  Notice deposited in the mail in the 
foregoing manner shall be deemed received five (5) days after it is so deposited, excluding 
Saturdays, Sundays, and postal holidays.  Notice given in any other manner shall be effective only 
if and when actually received by the party to be notified.  For purposes of notice, the addresses of 
the parties shall be as follows until changed as herein provided: 
 
Grantor: 
 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
2101 Oneil Ave. Room 101 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
 
Grantee: 
 
Samson Exploration, LLC  
 
 
 
 
ATTN: Surface Land Manager  
110 West 7th Street, Suite 2000  
Tulsa, Oklahoma 74119  
 
Either party may designate a different address for receipt of subsequent notices by notifying the 
other as provided in this paragraph. 
 
TO HAVE AND TO HOLD the above-described Easement unto Grantee, its successors 
and assigns, subject to all of the provisions and conditions hereinafter contained, for five (5) years 
following initial construction and for so long thereafter as some or all of the same shall be used for 
the purposes which are herein granted with no cessation of all such use for more than five (5) 
consecutive years. Grantor does hereby grant unto Grantee, its successors and assigns, the right to 
freely assign or otherwise convey all or part of Grantee’s interest in all or part of said Easement 
without obtaining the prior written or oral consent of Grantor.  If Grantee transfers its interest 
hereunder in whole or in part, Grantee shall be relieved of all obligations thereafter arising with 

 
respect to the transferred interest, and failure of the transferee to satisfy such obligations with 
respect to the transferred interest shall not affect the rights of Grantee with respect to any interest 
not so transferred. 
 
This instrument may be executed as one document or in several partially executed 
counterparts, and the original and counterparts shall be construed together and shall constitute one 
instrument.  The failure of one or more parties to execute this instrument or a counterpart hereof 
shall not in any manner affect the validity and binding effect of the same as to the parties who 
execute said instrument.  For recordation purposes, Grantee is authorized to detach the signature 
and acknowledgement pages from one or more counterparts and to attach them for filing with any 
other executed counterparts.

MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
 
 
This Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) is by and between the City of Cheyenne, a 
municipal corporation existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming (City), located at 2101 
O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, and Climb Wyoming, located at 1709 Carey 
Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001.  The City and Climb Wyoming are collectively referred to 
as “Party” or “Parties.”  This MOU shall become effective upon the date of the last signature 
affixed hereto. 
WHEREAS, Climb Wyoming is a statewide non-profit empowering low income, single 
moms by providing free training, mental health counseling and job placement in six (6) 
Wyoming communities including within the City of Cheyenne; 
 
WHEREAS, Climb Wyoming is currently operating a program in Cheyenne to train CDL 
drivers in Cheyenne; 
 
WHEREAS, the City is in need of individuals in possession of a valid Commercial 
Driver’s License (CDL) to work for a variety of positions within the City; and, 
 
WHEREAS, Climb Wyoming will provide the City with reimbursement for work 
performed by a Climb Wyoming participant including participant’s entry level salary and/or 
gross wages, including paid time off and holiday pay for non-overtime hours (40 hours or less 
per week) for a period of twelve (12) weeks. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the Parties agree as follows: 
 
1. 
Purpose.  The purpose of this MOU is for the City and Climb Wyoming to work 
together to provide employment opportunities for Climb Wyoming participants within the City 
of Cheyenne. 
3:47 pm, Mar 21 2023

 
Page 2 of 6 
 
2. 
Term. This MOU shall commence on the last date executed by the duly 
authorized representatives of the Parties to this MOU and shall remain in full force and effect 
until April 15, 2025, for a period of two (2) years. 
 
3. 
Payments.  Climb Wyoming agrees to provide payment to the City of Cheyenne 
pursuant to the terms and conditions of the Work Experience Agreement for each participant, a 
copy of which is attached and incorporated herein as Exhibit A.  
 
4. 
Responsibilities of Climb Wyoming.  Climb Wyoming shall: 
a. 
Identify work participants appropriate for employment within the City. 
b. 
Provide training for work participants including job placement and support 
within the City. 
c. 
Reimburse the City for work performed under this agreement based upon 
the actual cost incurred by the work experience participant’s entry level salary and/or 
gross wages, including paid time off and holiday pay for non-overtime hours (40 hours or 
less per week) of the work experience participant, with the exception that no 
reimbursement of employer paid retirement costs shall be paid from funds covered by the 
Work Experience Agreement. 
d. 
Provide support to the participant including by conducting two (2) Work 
Experience Performance Evaluations during the participant’s initial employment within 
the City. 
 
5. 
Responsibilities of the City. The City shall: 
a. 
The City shall provide employment and other services specified in, and 
consistent with, this Work Experience Agreement (Exhibit A). 
b. 
Provide documentation of amounts paid to participant. 

 
Page 3 of 6 
c. 
Coordinate with Climb Wyoming with regard to Work Experience 
Performance Evaluations. 
 
7. 
General Provisions. 
 
 
a. 
Amendments.  Any changes, modifications, revisions, or amendments to 
this MOU which are mutually agreed upon by the Parties to this MOU shall be 
incorporated by written instrument, executed and signed by all Parties to this MOU. 
b. 
Assignment.  No Party may assign or transfer the responsibilities or 
agreements made herein without the prior written consent of the non-assigning Party, 
which approval shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
c. 
Applicable Law.  The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this 
MOU shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of 
Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over this MOU and the Parties, and the venue shall be in 
the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
d. 
Compliance with Laws.  The Parties shall keep informed of and comply 
with all applicable federal, state, and local laws and regulations in the performance of this 
MOU. 
 e. 
Indemnification/Hold Harmless.  The Parties agree to indemnify, hold 
harmless, and defend the other from and against any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, 
forfeitures, and suits, and the cost and expenses incident thereto, including reasonable 
attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a result the performance of their duties, 
including death or bodily injury to any person, destruction or damage to any property, 
contamination of or adverse effects on the environment, or any violation of governmental 
laws, regulations, or orders. 

 
Page 4 of 6 
f. 
Nondiscrimination.  The Parties shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 
1964, the Wyoming Fair Employment Practices Act, Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq., the 
Americans With Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the 
Age Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations 
thereto, and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, 
creed, color, race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy, or qualifying disability 
in connection with the performance under this MOU. 
g. 
Governmental Immunity.  The City does not waive governmental 
immunity by entering into this MOU and specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., and all 
other applicable laws.  Designation of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and 
similar provisions should not be construed as a waiver of governmental immunity. 
h. 
Third-Party Beneficiary Rights.  The Parties do not intend to create in any 
other individual or entity, the status of third-party beneficiary and this MOU shall not be 
construed so as to create such status. The rights, duties, and obligations contained in this 
MOU shall operate only between the Parties to this MOU and shall inure solely to the 
benefit of the Parties to this MOU. The provisions of this MOU are intended only to 
assist the Parties in determining and performing their obligations under this MOU. The 
Parties to this MOU intend and expressly agree that only parties signatory to this MOU 
shall have any legal or equitable right to seek to enforce this MOU, to seek any remedy 
arising out of a Party’s performance or failure to perform any term or condition of this 
MOU, or to bring an action for the breach of this MOU. 
i. 
Force Majeure.  The performance of this MOU by the Parties shall be 

 
Page 5 of 6 
subject to force majeure including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural 
disaster, war or threat of war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized 
strikes, governmental regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by 
the World Health Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government 
authority or health agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-
19, H1N1, or similar infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other 
similar occurrence beyond the control of the Parties, where any of those factors, 
circumstances, situations, or conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or 
unreasonably delay the performance required by this MOU.  This MOU may be cancelled 
by any Party, without liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or 
amounts paid shall be refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written 
notice to the other Party. 
j. 
Severability.  If any provision of this MOU is found to be invalid or 
unenforceable for any reason, the remaining provisions will continue to be valid and 
enforceable.  If a court finds that any provision of this MOU is invalid or unenforceable, 
but that by limiting such provision it would become valid and enforceable, then such 
provision will be deemed to be written, construed, and enforced as so limited. 
k. 
Notice.  All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this MOU 
shall be in writing and given to the Party either by regular mail or delivery in person. 
l. 
Termination.  Any Party may terminate its participation in this MOU, with 
or without cause, by providing thirty (30) days written notice to the other Party. 
m. 
Prior Approval.  This MOU shall not be binding upon the Parties, no 
services shall be performed under the terms of this MOU, and no payments shall be made 

 
Page 6 of 6 
until this MOU has been reduced to writing and approved by all necessary authorities. 
n. 
Entirety of Contract.  This MOU represents the entire and integrated 
agreement between the Parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, representations, and 
agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
8. 
Signatures.  In witness whereof, the Parties to this MOU, through their duly 
authorized representatives, have executed this MOU on the days and dates set out below and 
certify that they have read, understood, and knowingly and voluntarily agreed to the terms and 
conditions of this MOU.

MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
Between 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND 
THE CHILDREN’S MUSEUM OF CHEYENNE 
 
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into by and between the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, a municipal corporation organized under the laws of the State of Wyoming, located at 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 (“CITY”), and the Children’s Museum of 
Cheyenne, located at P.O. Box 92, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82003 (“Children’s Museum”). 
 
I. 
PURPOSE 
 
 
Pursuant to Resolution No. #6316, attached and incorporated herein with supporting 
documentation consisting of a total of four (4) pages, the purpose of this Agreement is for the 
CITY to pay the Children’s Museum for a portion of the of the costs for financial grant 
consideration for the Children’s Museum of Cheyenne Project in accordance with the Proposition 
#10 of the 2021 voluntary one percent (1%) specific purpose sales and use tax (“6th penny tax”) 
related to the development of downtown improvements and attractions. 
 
II. 
TERM 
 
 
This Agreement shall commence on the date last executed by the duly authorized 
representatives of the parties to this Agreement and shall remain in full force and effect until June 
1, 2024 or terminated as provided herein. 
 
III. 
RESPONSIBILITIES OF THE CITY 
 
 
The CITY shall pay the Children’s Museum up to Two Hundred Eighty-Eight Thousand, 
Two Hundred Fifty Dollars ($288,250.00) from the 2021 voluntary one percent (1%) specific 
purpose sales and use tax (“6th penny tax”), upon presentation of a properly executed invoice and 
accounting as prescribed by the City Treasurer.  The CITY’s payment obligation under this 
Agreement is capped at Two Hundred Eighty-Eight Thousand, Two Hundred Fifty Dollars 
($288,250.00).  Payments shall be made in accordance with Wyo. Stat. § 16-6-602 (as amended). 
 
IV. 
RESPONSIBILITIES OF THE CHILDREN’S MUSEUM 
 
 
A. 
The Children’s Museum agrees that the funds paid by the CITY pursuant to this 
Agreement shall not be for the express aid of any private citizen, or in violation of Article 16, 
Section 6 of the Constitution of the State of Wyoming. 
 
B. 
The Children’s Museum agrees to provide an invoice to the CITY consisting at a 
minimum of detailed accounting describing the costs and services provided pursuant to this 
Agreement. 
 
11:49 am, Apr 19 2023

 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne 
and the Children’s Museum of Cheyenne 
Page 2 of 5 
C. 
The Children’s Museum agrees to maintain generally accepted accounting 
procedures and practices, and to maintain books, records, documents, and other evidence to 
sufficiently and properly reflect all transactions of any nature relating to this Agreement or any 
funds provided by or through the CITY including, but not limited to, the funds referenced herein.  
Any books, records, documents, and other evidence shall be made available upon written request 
of the CITY for the inspection at the offices of the Children’s Museum in Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
D. 
The Children’s Museum shall provide documentation that the project has gone 
through a public procurement process that generally complies to the greatest extent possible the 
intent of Wyoming Statute, including §15-1-113.  The Children’s Museum agrees to provide 
appropriate documentation, including, but not limited to paid invoices, bidding documents, 
solicitation documents, and procurement documents. 
 
 
E.  
The Children’s Museum agrees to retain all required records for three (3) years after 
the CITY makes final payment and all other matters relating to the Agreement are concluded.  The 
Children’s Museum agrees to permit access by the CITY or any of its duly authorized 
representatives to any books, documents, papers, and records of the Children’s Museum which are 
directly pertinent to this specific Agreement for purposes including, but not limited to, audit, 
examination, excerpts, and transcriptions.  It is agreed that finished or unfinished documents, data, 
or reports, prepared by the Children’s Museum under this Agreement shall be considered the 
property of the CITY and upon completion of the services to be performed, or upon termination 
of this Agreement for cause, or for the convenience of the CITY, will be turned over to the CITY. 
 
V. 
GENERAL PROVISIONS 
 
 
A. 
Independent Contractor.  The services to be performed by the Children’s Museum 
are those of an independent Contractor and not as an employee of CITY.  The Children’s Museum 
is not eligible for City of Cheyenne Employee benefits and will be treated as an independent 
contractor for federal tax filing purposes.  The Children’s Museum assumes responsibility for its 
personnel who provide services pursuant to this Agreement and will make all deductions required 
of employers by state, federal, and local laws and shall maintain liability insurance for each of 
them.  The Children’s Museum is free to perform the same or similar services for others. 
 
 
B. 
Acceptance Not Waiver. CITY’s approval of the reports and work or materials 
furnished hereunder shall not in any way relieve the Children’s Museum of responsibility for the 
technical accuracy of the work.  CITY’s approval or acceptance of, or payment for, any of the 
services shall not be construed to operate as a waiver of any rights under this Agreement or of any 
cause of action arising out of the performance of this Agreement. 
 
 
C. 
Termination.  This Agreement may be terminated (a) by either party at any time for 
failure of the other party to comply with the terms and conditions of this agreement; (b) by either 
party with thirty (30) days’ prior written notice to the other party; or (c) upon mutual written 
agreement by both parties. 

 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne 
and the Children’s Museum of Cheyenne 
Page 3 of 5 
 
 
D. 
Entire Agreement.  This Agreement consisting of five (5) pages plus supporting 
documentation consisting of four (4) pages represents the entire and integrated agreement and 
understanding between the parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, statements, 
representations, and agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
 
E. 
Assignment.  Neither this Agreement nor any rights or obligations hereunder shall 
be assigned or delegated by a party without the prior written consent of the other party. 
 
 
F. 
Modification.  This Agreement shall be modified only by a written agreement, duly 
executed by all parties hereto. 
 
 
G. 
Invalidity.  If any provision of this Agreement is held invalid or unenforceable by 
any court of competent jurisdiction, or if the CITY is advised of any such actual or potential 
invalidity or unenforceability, such holding or advice shall not invalidate or render unenforceable 
any other provision hereof.  It is the express intent of the parties that the provisions of this 
Agreement are fully severable. 
 
 
H. 
Applicable Law and Venue.  The parties mutually understand and agree this 
Agreement shall be governed by and interpreted pursuant to the laws of the State of Wyoming, 
without regard to its conflict of laws principles.  If any dispute arises between the parties from or 
concerning this Agreement or the subject matter hereof, any suit or proceeding at law or in equity 
shall be brought in the District Court of the State of Wyoming, First Judicial District, sitting at 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  The foregoing provisions of this paragraph are agreed by the parties to be 
a material inducement to the Children’s Museum and to CITY in executing this Agreement.  This 
provision is not intended nor shall it be construed to waive CITY’s governmental immunity as 
provided in this Agreement. 
 
 
I. 
Contingencies. The Children’s Museum certifies and warrants no gratuities, 
kickbacks, or contingency fees were paid in connection with this Agreement, nor were any fees, 
commissions, gifts, or other considerations made contingent upon the award of this Agreement. 
 
 
J. 
Discrimination.  All parties agree they will not discriminate against any person who 
performs work under the terms and conditions of this Agreement because of race, color, gender, 
creed, handicapping condition, or national origin. 
 
 
K. 
ADA Compliance.  All parties agree they will not discriminate against a qualified 
individual with disability, pursuant to a law as set forth in the Americans with Disabilities Act, 
P.L. 101-336, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and/or any properly promulgated rules and regulations 
relating thereto. 
 
 
L. 
Governmental/Sovereign 
Immunity: 
CITY 
does 
not 
waive 
its 
Governmental/Sovereign Immunity, as provided by any applicable law including Wyo. Stat. § 1-

 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne 
and the Children’s Museum of Cheyenne 
Page 4 of 5 
39-101, et seq., by entering into this Agreement.  Further, CITY fully retains all immunities and 
defenses provided by law with regard to any action, whether in tort, contract, or any other theory 
of law, based on this Agreement. 
 
 
M. 
Indemnification.  To the fullest extent permitted by law, the Children’s Museum 
agrees to indemnify and hold harmless CITY, its elected and appointed officials, employees, and 
volunteers from any and all liability for injuries, damages, claims, penalties, actions, demands, or 
expenses arising solely from or in connection with the work that is to be performed by or on behalf 
of the Children’s Museum for CITY except to the extent liability is caused by the sole negligence 
or willful misconduct of CITY, its elected and appointed officials, employees, and volunteers.  The 
Children’s Museum shall carry liability insurance sufficient to cover its obligations under this 
provision and provide CITY with proof of such insurance. 
 
 
N.  
Third Parties.  The parties do not intend to create in any other individual or entity 
the status of third-party beneficiary, and this Agreement shall not be construed so as to create such 
status.  The rights, duties, and obligations contained in this Agreement shall operate only between 
the parties to the Agreement and shall inure solely to the benefit of the parties to this Agreement.  
 
 
O. 
Conflict of Interest.  CITY and the Children’s Museum affirms, to their knowledge, 
that no Children’s Museum employee has any personal beneficial interest whatsoever in the 
Agreement described herein.  No staff member of the Children’s Museum, compensated either 
partially or wholly with funds from this Agreement, shall engage in any conduct or activity which 
would constitute a conflict of interest relative to this Agreement. 
 
 
P. 
Force Majeure.  The performance of the Agreement by either party shall be subject 
to force majeure including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural disaster, war or threat 
of war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized strikes, governmental regulation 
or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by the World Health Organization, the Centers 
for Disease Control, or local government authority or health agencies (including, but not limited 
to, the health threats of COVID-19, H1N1, or similar infectious diseases), curtailment of 
transportation facilities, or other similar occurrence beyond the control of the parties, where any 
of those factors, circumstances, situations, or conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or 
unreasonably delay the performance required by this Agreement. The Agreement may be cancelled 
by either party, without liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or amounts 
paid shall be refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written notice to the other 
party. 
 
 
Q. 
Limitation on Payment.  CITY’s payment obligation is conditioned upon the 
availability of funds which are appropriated, allocated, or approved by the Governing Body for the 
payment of this obligation.  If funds are not allocated, appropriated, or approved and available, in 
the fiscal judgment of the CITY, for the continuance of the services and equipment provided by 
the Children’s Museum, the Agreement may be terminated by CITY at the end of the period for 
which funds are available.  No penalty shall accrue to CITY in the event this provision is exercised, 

 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne 
and the Children’s Museum of Cheyenne 
Page 5 of 5 
and CITY shall not be obligated or liable for any future payments due or for any damages as a 
result of termination under this provision. 
 
 
R. 
Notices.  All notices required and permitted under this Agreement shall be deemed 
to have been given, if and when deposited in the U.S. Mail, properly stamped and addressed to the 
party for whom intended at such party’s address listed herein, or when personally delivered 
personally to such party. A party may change its address for notice hereunder by giving written 
notice to the other party. 
 
 
S.  
Compliance with Laws.  The Children’s Museum shall comply with all applicable 
federal, state, or local laws, regulations, and ordinances. 
 
 
In witness whereof, the parties to this Agreement, through their duly authorized 
representatives, have executed this Agreement on the days and dates set out below and certify that 
they have read, understood, and agreed to the terms and conditions of this Agreement. 
 
 
This Agreement is effective the date of the last signature affixed to this page.

APPROVED as to form only:
By Stefanie Baster at 3:53 pm, Mar 242023
RESOLUTION NO.
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION EXPRESSING THE WILL OF THE GOVERNING
BODY TO CONSIDER SPECIFIED DOWNTOWN 6TH PENNY TAX
PROJECT FUNDS FOR INFRASTRUCTURE ENHANCEMENTS FOR
THE CHILDREN’S MUSEUM OF CHEYENNE.”
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne has certain funds available for downtown Cheyenne
maintenance and improvement projects through the 2021 voluntary one percent (1%) specific
purpose sales and use tax (“6th penny tax”) approved by the affected citizenry of Laramie County;
WHEREAS, Proposition #10 of the 6th penny tax provided that: “$6,017,966 to City of
Cheyenne and the interest earned thereon for downtown improvements and maintenance project
to improve and maintain infrastructure such as roads, parking, curbs, gutters, sidewalks, lighting
and alleys in the DDA District; to operate and maintain the Depot Plaza”;
WHEREAS, literature concerning Proposition #10 of the 6th penny tax informed the voter
of the following facts:
•
Ninety-one percent (91%) of Cheyenne and Laramie County residents have
vocalized wanting to see efforts that actively work to improve Downtown
Cheyenne;
•
Sixty-eight percent (68%) ofCheyenne and Laramie County visitors have identified
“Experiencing Downtown”
as their top activity to do in Cheyenne which
contributes to sales tax revenue benefits for all of Lararnie County;
•
Infrastructure improvements have an estimated cost increase of four percent (4%)
each year, which
impacts the bottom-line expenses for necessary repairs,
maintenance, and improvements that benefit the entire community; and,
•
As the heart of the Capital City of Wyoming, Downtown Cheyenne is the center of
social, economic, and cultural development;
WHEREAS, according to a 2018 Association of Children’s Museums’ study, “The
Economic Impact of Children’s Museums: The Ripple Effect of Spending,” children’s museums
created $5.5 billion in economic activity in 2016. The study, produced in collaboration with Knew
Knowledge Organization Ltd, and funded by the Institute of Museum and Library Service,
emphasized how impressive this $5.5 billion figure given that it was generated by a $1.5 billion
investment, with more than three hundred fifty percent (350%) return. Based on those numbers,
the Children’s Museum of Cheyenne’s initial $2.2 million investment will eventually equate to
roughly $8 million in Cheyenne and the surrounding area;
WHEREAS, Costs provided by the Children’s Museum of Cheyenne provided in the
Construction Manager At Risk (CMAR) estimated Costs table and map, attached as (Appendix A)

will reflect that the Children’s Museum of Cheyenne meets the requirements stated under
Proposition #10 of the 6th penny tax; and,
WHEREAS, with approval of this resolution the Cheyenne City Attorney’s Office will
introduce a contract at the following City Council meeting to meet all stipulations of the City of
Cheyenne purchasing requirements and legal requirements regarding the transfer of funds to the
Children’s Museum of Cheyenne from Proposition #10 of the 6th penny tax.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Governing Body through issuance of this Resolution
appropriates authorized 6th penny tax funding in an amount not to exceed Two Hundred Eighty-
Eight Thousand, Two Hundred Fifty Dollars ($288,250.00) for the purposes specified herein.
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this
______
day of
2023.
Patrick Collins, Mayor
